LONG GUNS/RIFLES 
Long guns or riﬂes are designed to hit at much longer 
ranges than handguns, often with additional stopping 
power. They are standard weapons of the Space Corps 
and the Citi‐Def militia and are also popular with 
settlers in the Cursed Earth and oﬀ‐world colonies. 
Their value is also recognised by lawless elements in 
Mega‐City One where they are seen as eﬀective assault 
weapons and, in the hands of a trained marksman, a 
potential Judge Killer.  
The basic Combat Riﬂe is a robust, simple‐to‐use long 
range ﬁrearm. Its caseless ammunition is easy to procure 
and the gun itself requires little maintenance. Mega‐City 
gangs have found it to be useful both as an ambush 
weapon and in block‐to‐block long‐range ﬁreﬁghts.

The Sniper Riﬂe is a much‐modiﬁed military riﬂe ﬁtted 
with an extended barrel for longer range. The integral 
sniping scope makes this a highly accurate weapon 
capable of taking out most targets with a well‐aimed 
shot. This weapon is generally only given to the most 
skilled of marksmen who usually ﬁre it from a well‐hidden 
vantage point. It’s one failing is the clumsiness of the long 
barrel which makes it diﬃcult to use when not properly 
set up. However, any sniper foolish enough to allow an 
enemy to get close is likely to have a short career. 
No weapon embodies the Citi‐Def more than the 
infamous Spit Carbine. History has shown that urban 
battles are mostly fought at close quarters where subtle 
tactics come second to simple ﬁrepower. The Spit Carbine 
can trace its ancestry back to the famous 20th century 
urban combat weapons like the Thompson, PPSh‐41 and 
the MP40. These weapons were designed to be used by 
moderately‐trained troops to pin down and suppress 
enemies with a blizzard of incoming ﬁre. Such a weapon 
is perfectly suited to Citi‐Def training and tactics.  
The Laser Riﬂe is basically a scaled‐up version of the 
laser pistol with superior optics and high‐power 
capacitors that enable targets to be hit at long range with 
a powerful pulse of coherent light. Whilst much more 
eﬀective than a standard riﬂe, lasers are diﬃcult to 
acquire, fetch a high price and are hard to maintain 
without specialist tools and training.

STUMP GUNS 
Stump Guns can trace their ancestry back to the venerable 
shotgun and are potent short‐range weapons that excel in 
close quarter combat. Their main drawbacks are the bulky 
ammo and requirement to frequently reload. 
The standard Stump Gun features a wide bore, a small 
magazine and chunky rounds that ﬁre clouds of pellets, 
ﬂechettes or, in more exotic cases, monoﬁlament‐wire or 
miniature fragmentation charges. Stump guns are generally 
easier to manufacture and maintain than many other long 
arms and their impressive short‐range capabilities make 
them favoured weapons for armed criminals. 
A few minutes with a hacksaw can convert a standard 
stump gun into a Sawn‐oﬀ by shortening the barrel. The 
result is a compact weapon that can be ﬁred one‐handed 
but retains the stump gun’s stopping power. 
Whilst retaining all the attributes of a standard stump 
gun the Auto Stump has a higher rate of ﬁre and a 
magazine containing additional cartridges reducing the 
need to reload so regularly.

HEAVY WEAPONS 
War‐reserve heavy weapons are more commonly used by 
the Justice Department and the Citi‐Def during Def‐Con 
exercises or when a threat worthy of their use emerges 
(in Mega‐City One this tends to be a surprisingly 
common event!). The availability of these weapons to the 
Citi‐Def is a two‐edged sword: while it provides the city 
with a well‐armed defensive militia, the inherently 
unstable nature of the Citi‐Def causes escalation 
problems. It is not unknown for a member to take a 
rocket launcher home for the weekend to impress family 
and friends or to explosively settle a personal score with 
a rival block. Through this and many other routes, heavy 
weapons can end up in the wrong hands and can become 
a feature in arms races between rival gangs.  
For those who like some additional ﬁrepower heavy 
weapons are an attractive proposition, although they 
can be cumbersome in a close‐range combat. 
The following rules apply to all heavy weapons: 
• Aimed Fire only. 
• Reduces the model’s Move stat by ‐1". 
• Reduces the model’s Evade stat by ‐1 (can never 
be reduced below 0).

The rocket can be conﬁgured as an anti‐vehicle direct ﬁre 
or anti‐infantry blast weapon. A few HAWKs have found 
their way into the hands of criminal organisations with 
enough credits to grease the palms of the right people.  
The use of special rounds requires the expenditure of 
speciﬁc Armoury cards:

CLOSE COMBAT WEAPONS 
While plenty of fights in Mega‐City One are decided 
with guns many more are resolved up close and 
personal. Indeed, the real crazies seem to enjoy 
looking into their opponent’s eyes as 
they fight. Gangs, especially those 
composed of Juves, regularly 
become involved in huge 
rumbles with rival gangs 
where firearms are not 
permitted. Outwardly this is 
a form of bravado, but 
actually it is born out of fear 
of a faster and deadlier Justice 
Department intervention if 
firearms are reported.  
MARIA 
Perhaps Mega-City One's most enthusiastic 
employer of the Culinary Laser was Maria. 
Employed as Judge Dredd’s housekeeper for 
many years, she is a skilled cook and used this 
tool regularly to prepare meals for the dour 
lawman. During Block Mania, Maria, like so many 
other citizens, was affected by the aggression 
enhancing toxin that ravaged the city. All her 
pent-up anger was let loose as she looked for 
anyone to fight. She was seriously under-
estimated as a threat by several Sov-Judges 
who messily paid with their lives as they fell to 
her deftly handled Culinary Laser. 
A Basic Close Combat Weapon could be a club, 
knuckledusters, axe, or just about anything else sharp 
and/or sturdy enough to swing and do harm with. 
Whilst a massively popular consumer item for Citizens 
addicted to the reality programs ‘My Block Kitchen Rules’ 
and ‘Megacitychef’, Culinary Lasers used for cutting and 
heating of foodstuﬀs have an unfortunate ﬂaw. A few 
simple, highly illegal modiﬁcations allow the laser blade 
to be turned into an easy‐to‐hide, deadly weapon.

brought on by the strains of life in the

usually become violent and dangerous

WEAPON SPECIAL RULES 
Weapon special rules are used to show diﬀerences 
between the hand guns and riﬂes used by the Perps 
and Judges.  
AIMED FIRE ONLY  
Some bulkier weapons or those with slow rates of  
ﬁre need to be set up in order to ﬁre, loaded one round 
at a time, or take time to charge before each shot. 
These weapons may only be used if the model uses an 
Aimed Fire double‐action. However, unlike other 
weapons, they gain no extra bonuses from using an 
Aimed Fire action (i.e. they don’t add +2 to either Shoot 
or Power). Aimed Fire weapons always have a Focused 
Front (FF) ﬁre arc. 
BLAST (X")  
Blast weapons are ones that use explosives, ﬂames, 
chemicals or other energies to aﬀect an area. Blast 
weapons take eﬀect on all models within X" of the point 
that they hit and can be ﬁred at models or just a point 
of the ground.  
Resolve a hit against each model in the area of eﬀect 
using the weapon’s Power stat. A Blast weapon’s Power 
stat is normally reduced by 1 for each inch a model is 
away from the target point although there are 
exceptions to this like Flame and Gas weapons. 
Blast weapon scatter 
Failing to roll any [HITS] with a Blast weapon shot 
means the target has been missed, but the shot will still 
land somewhere nearby.  
Roll a Combat die: 
The shot is a dud or otherwise ineﬀective. 
The shot lands D3+3" beyond the  
intended target. 
The shot lands D3+3" short of the  
intended target. 
Resolve any damage caused from the landing point for 
the shot. 
Example: A perp aims a hi‐ex bomb (Blast 3) at a 
judge 4" away. The perp fails to roll any [HITS] – 
so a Combat die is rolled to determine where 
the shot lands. A [SPECIAL] result is rolled, 
and the perp then rolls D3+3, scoring a 6, 
which results in the shot landing 6" short 
of the intended target. The bomb ends 
up 1" behind the unlucky perp, and he 
is caught in the resulting blast.

Electric shock weapons pack a high voltage charge.

INCENDIARY  
Incendiary or ﬂame weapons negate any positive Resist 
modiﬁers for cover. If an attack from a Flame weapon 
would result in the target model being pinned it is also 
driven D6" directly away from the model making the 
attack and stunned after any damage from the hit has 
been resolved. 
Flame weapons with the Blast rule (see opposite) do 
not reduce their Power against models further away 
from the centre of the blast. In the case of ﬂame blasts, 
if the target model is pinned it is driven away D6" from 
the centre of the blast and stunned after any damage 
from the hit has been resolved.  
GAS 
Gas weapons with the Blast rule do not reduce their 
Power against models further away from the centre of 
the blast. Models aﬀected by Gas automatically gain a 
Pinned marker in addition to any other eﬀects. 
Leave a marker representing the area of the gas cloud 
in place on the tabletop and roll a Combat die for each 
cloud at the start of each subsequent model activation.  
= The gas cloud dissipates and is removed 
= The gas cloud stays in place and will 
take eﬀect against any model that 
enters it, and any models still inside  
it, at the end of the active model’s activation. Resolve 
this as another hit with the gas weapon. 
KNOCKBACK 
If an attack from a Knockback weapon is not evaded the 
target model is driven D6" directly away from the 
model making the attack in addition to any damage 
inﬂicted. Stump Gun pellets or baton rounds typically 
cause Knockback eﬀects.  
LASER 
High energy laser weapons can cut through the thickest 
armour like a knife through butter. Their downside is a 
slow recharge rate. A model must use the Aimed Fire 
action to shoot with a laser weapon. In addition, a  laser 
weapon has the Piercing special rule.  
LONG BARrEL 
Long barrels are the trademark upgrade for sniper or 
hunting riﬂes. Range is much improved, but the 
resulting awkwardness of the barrel makes the riﬂe 
almost useless for quickly aiming. 
armoury
A model shooting a weapon with the Long Barrel 
special rule suﬀers a ‐2 modiﬁer when taking the 
Snapshot action. 
PIERCING 
A target hit by a weapon with the Piercing special rule 
reduces its Resist against the attack by ‐1. This modiﬁer 
is cumulative with the Rending special rule. 
RENDING 
A target hit by a weapon with the Rending special rule 
reduces its Resist against the attack by ‐1. This modiﬁer 
is cumulative with the Piercing special rule. 
STUN  
When making a ranged or close combat attack with a 
Stun weapon, the attacking model has the option to 
use the weapon for a non‐lethal takedown instead of a 
lethal attack. 
If the option is taken, the Stun weapon will 
always cause at least one stunned result on 
the Injury Chart regardless of the 
modiﬁed Power for a hit (i.e. even if 
the total is negative and wouldn’t 
normally have any eﬀect). 
Furthermore, it counts all 
injury results as 
additional stunned 
results instead.  
THROW  
Close combat weapons 
with the Throw rule can 
be hurled at targets up 
to 6" away using the 
Throw action. However, 
use the attacking 
model's Fight stat to 
determine the Power of 
the attack, just as if it was 
a close combat attack (see 
page 25).  
Other weapons, such as bombs, 
with the Throw special rule can 
only use the Throw action, instead of 
Snapshot or Aimed Fire, in order  
to make a ranged attack.

## PDF Page 52

50
ARMOURY CARDS 
Whilst most ﬁghting in Mega‐City One is 
conducted with basic guns and various sharp or 
blunt implements, certain non‐standard and limited‐
use weapons such as grenades, special ammunition and 
other more exotic combat equipment will sometimes 
make an appearance. These are handled with Armoury 
cards. These can add to the tension of the game by 
adding an element of the unknown – does that 
cornered perp just have muscle backing them up? Or do 
they have a lethal surprise waiting?  
Armoury cards also cover some equipment that is only 
circumstantially useful but generally available. In these 
cases, having the right Armoury cards can represent a 
model being quick witted enough to make a call in a 
combat situation.  
A good example of this kind of eﬀect is respirators; all 
Judges have respirators built into their helmets for 
emergency use, but Judges are still aﬀected by airborne 
perils like gas with surprising frequency. The truth is a 
Judge may not have his or her helmet on (excepting 
Judge Dredd, obviously), may not be fast enough in 
recognising the danger and getting the respirator into 
place, or ﬁnd that their respirator has been damaged or is 
simply ineﬀective against the danger – all these 
possibilities are taken into account by needing an 
Armoury card to use respirators instead of simply ruling 
Judges are immune to gas. Likewise, the chance of a 
Judge having Hi‐Ex or Hot Shot ammo for their Lawgiver, 
and thinking it appropriate to use it, are covered by 
requiring an appropriate card to take the shot. 
GAINING ARMOURY CARDS 
At the start of a game deal six Armoury cards to each 
player. A player may select and keep up to three 
Armoury cards but must discard the rest.  
Note some special skills allow characters to draw and/or 
keep additional Armoury cards.  
For example, a Citi‐Def Squad 
Leader has the Access to the 
Armoury skill. If the Citi‐Def 
Squad Leader is leading a 
faction, the player will draw 
one additional Armoury card 
(seven in total) and is allowed 
to keep one more card than 
usual (making it four). 
PLAYING ARMOURY CARDS 
Armoury cards can be played on any friendly model 
(i.e. they aren’t assigned to speciﬁc models). Most 
Armoury cards can only be used by an active model 
performing a speciﬁc action listed on the card. While a 
few take eﬀect at the beginning during the Set Up or 
can be used to react to speciﬁc enemy actions. 
Special rules speciﬁc to some Armoury cards are listed 
on the following pages.

ARMOUR & SPECIALIST EQUIPMENT 
Ricochet  
Ricochet rounds have a special coating and are ﬁred at low 
velocity so they will bounce oﬀ walls, ﬂoors and ceilings 
potentially hitting multiple targets. They are highly 
eﬀective in conﬁned spaces and against targets in cover. 
A weapon ﬁring ricochet rounds gains Blast 2" and no 
longer needs a line of ﬁre in order to hit targets as the 
rounds can be literally bounced around corners. Any 
Resist bonus for cover is ignored. If the target is inside a 
conﬁned space (i.e. one smaller than Blast 2" can ﬁt 
inside) increase weapon’s Power for the hit by +2. 
Seeker  
Missiles or projectiles with the capability of homing in on a 
target are relatively old tech. However, sophisticated seekers 
capable of detecting body heat through walls or other 
obstructions have now been miniaturised. The weapon is 
aimed towards the suspected position of the target, allowing 
the warhead to ‘sniﬀ’ out thermal patterns that match the 
output of a desired engagement target. On acquiring the 
target, the round is ﬁred and uses tiny reaction jets to 
manoeuvre around obstacles and hit its mark. 
A Seeker Armoury card may be used for a single shot with 
a Rocket Launcher or a Lawgiver pistol. Sophisticated 
homing rounds use special sensors and variable thrust 
propulsion to achieve extremely high levels of accuracy. 
The classic example is the Lawgiver Heat Seeker round 
which locks onto the body heat of its target. 
A Seeker round can be fired at a target beyond normal 
range and will hit even a target out of sight of the firer. 
Add 8" to a weapon’s long range stat if it is firing a 
Seeker, and ignore any weapon range modifiers. No 
line of fire is required to attack a target with a Seeker 
(assuming they are not inside a sealed space) but if the 
target is not visible to the attacker a ‐1 shoot penalty 
is applied.  
A weapon with the seeker rule is hard to dodge;  
count as ‐1 to the target’s Evade stat. For example, a 
character with an Evade of 2 will only roll 1 dice to 
dodge a Seeker. Any Resist bonus for cover is also 
reduced by ‐1. 
The up/downside to a Seeker is that if the shot misses 
or is dodged it will automatically hit a diﬀerent model 
within 3" of the original target, randomise which model 
is hit from those available.  
Stealth Suit 
This is a form ﬁtting bodysuit constructed from a special 
light‐absorbing material allowing its wearer to hide in the 
shadows. The suit also masks the heat signature of the 
human body making detection with peepers or other 
image intensifying devices diﬃcult. 
Once played on a model a Stealth Suit remains in play 
for the rest of the game, granting the model +1 to its 
Evade stat. In addition, the model cannot be spotted in 
scenarios with guards or targeted by Overwatch attacks 
until it has made a ranged or close combat attack for 
the ﬁrst time in the game. 
Citi-Def scour the rooftops of Karl Urban 
block for a reported Bat Glider intruder.

## PDF Page 58

BIG MEG CARDS 
Judges and criminals alike know that nothing 
ever goes smoothly on a job, especially in the Big 
Meg. Whether due to the vagaries of weather control, 
opposition schemes or simply the winds of fortune life 
is always unpredictable. Big Meg cards represent these 
wrinkles that make each story unique, as well as 
showing oﬀ some of the more colourful (and 
dangerous) aspects of life in Mega‐City One. 
GAINING BIG MEG CARDS 
Players can use Big Meg to gain an advantage during 
the game. Each player draws three Big Meg cards 
during the Set Up that can be played during the game. 
Some characters and scenarios may grant additional 
Big Meg cards. 
 
USING BIG MEG CARDS 
Unless specified otherwise on the card, Big Meg cards 
are used during the Encounter part of a scenario. Big 
Meg cards can be played as detailed on the card or 
discarded in other circumstances to gain another 
benefit (for an example see Quick Thinking). The 
player always chooses which card is discarded. 
Most Big Meg cards can be played between activations 
– that is, after one model has resolved its actions but 
before the next chip is drawn. Unless speciﬁed on the 
card they cannot be used to interrupt an activation 
once it has begun. 
Some Big Meg cards are best played at the start of 
the turn before the first activation, others may be 
played during the turn. Big Meg cards can be played 
on any model, even one that has already activated 
this turn. 
UNDERDOG BIG MEG CARDS 
A faction with a lower starting Notoriety than their 
opponent may gain extra Big Meg cards at the start of 
the game to help balance their chances. For every full 
10 points of Notoriety less than the other side, rounding 
up, the underdog gains an extra Big Meg card. 
QUICK THINKING 
At the start of any turn, when the Action chips 
are being placed in the bag, a player may 
choose to discard a Big Meg card to put in an 
extra Action chip of their colour.

MEGA-CITY ONE – CHRONOLOGY  
2023: America, with a population approaching a 
billion people, starts the Mega-City programme. 
2047: Mega-City One is considered complete. 
However, it is soon realised that current policing 
cannot handle the scale of crime, as Gangs based 
around the old city areas begin to form. 
2051: Chief Judge Solomon appointed and Judge 
Morton Judd outlines his ideas for cloning Judges. 
2066: Accelerated cloning begins and the first 
clones, Joe and Rico Dredd, are created. 
2070: President Robert L Booth starts the  
Atomic Wars. The USA, Russia, and Europe are 
devastated but the Mega-Cities survive due to  
their laser defences. 
2071: All lands between the Mega Cities are 
converted into a burned radioactive hell which 
becomes known as the Cursed Earth. Booth’s 
robotic Presidential Guard is defeated at the Battle 
of Armageddon. 
2072: The flow of refugees boosts Mega-City One’s 
population to 550 million. 
2079: The Dredd brothers graduate from the 
Academy of Justice to become full Judges. 
2088: Luna-1 is established on the Moon. This is a 
joint venture by the North American Mega-Cities. 
2099: Call-Me-Kenneth sparks the First Robot War.  
2100: Mega-City Two is infected by the deadly 
2T(FRU)T virus. Dredd leads a team across the 
Cursed Earth to deliver the vaccine. 
2101: Mad Judge Cal of the SJS takes control of Mega-
City One but is eventually brought down by Dredd. 
2102: Judge Death’s first arrival in Mega-City One.   
2104: Sov Agent Orlok spreads the Block Mania virus 
bringing the city to its knees and ripe for invasion 
by East-Meg One in the Apocalypse War. Mega-City 
One prevails and East-Meg One is destroyed. Big 
Meg casualties are over 400 million. 
2109: The Latest Democracy movement is crushed by 
the Justice Department.  
2112: The Dark Judges assisted by the Sisters of 
Death take over Mega-City One, converting it into 
their Necropolis domain. Once again Dredd and 
Anderson are instrumental in saving the city. 
2114: Sabbat, a powerful Necromancer from the 
future, creates a huge zombie horde in his attempt 
to conquer the Earth. Many Mega-Cities are nuked 
to stop the spread. Billions die worldwide yet Mega-
City One endures. 
2115: The first Mechanismo Robot Auxiliary Judges 
are deployed to the streets to make up for the 
scarcity of Judges. Initial results are not good. 
2121: The cyborg crime lord Nero Narcos initiates the 
Doomsday Scenario, with the assistance of Orlok and a 
robot army, to take over Mega-City One. The Justice 
Department is crippled when sabotaged Lawgiver 
pistols self-destruct. Nero is finally taken down by 
Dredd and a team which includes Brit-Cit judges.  
2124: Orlok the Assassin finally recaptured  
and executed. 
2130: Anti-mutant laws repealed, bringing an  
influx of mutie immigrants into the city. Mass 
rioting ensues. 
2134: For 30 years East-Meg One survivors have 
been planning revenge on Mega-City One for the 
destruction of their city. The so-called Fourth 
Faction unleashes the Chaos Bug on the city. In the 
space of a few hours the population turns upon 
itself in a vast orgy of murder and destruction. The 
Justice Department can do nothing as nearly 350 
million die.   
2136-2138: The Titan penal colony revolt. Insurrection 
in the off-world colonies and the great space war 
against the alien Zhind.  
2141: A resurgent  
Mega-City One population 
is now 130 million. New 
Chief Judge Logan 
appoints Robot 
Auxiliary Judge 
Harvey to the 
Council of Five.

## PDF Page 60

THE JUSTICE DEPARTMENT  
THE JUDGES 
STREeT JUDGES 
“Nobody’s innocent, citizen. We’re just here to determine 
the level of your guilt.” 
To the average Mega‐City One inhabitant, the most 
obvious symbol of the power and authority of the 
Justice Department is the Street Judge. These 
formidable individuals are responsible for the strict 
enforcement of law and order on the dangerous streets 
of the Big Meg. They have the authority to act as 
judge, jury and, if necessary, executioner. Crimes 
ranging from simple littering to ﬁrst‐degree murder fall 
within their remit. The punishments of a perpetrator 
can vary from a stern word of admonition, a crack on 
the head with a day stick, to imprisonment in an 
isolation cube or, in the most serious cases, a Lawgiver 
Execution round.  
Every new Street Judge is given a sector to patrol for  
a minimum of four years, although temporary 
reassignment is often necessary to shore up other 
understaﬀed sectors. In the early months these 
probationary Judges will still be assigned a mentor, 
usually a senior Street Judge, who will check in with 
them on a regular basis and provide guidance and 
advice where needed. At the end of the four year period 
they may be then transferred to a new sector for their 
second term. The Department has often real concerns 
that serving too long in the same sector can promote 
complacency on the part of the Judge. On the positive 
side, a new sector will provide fresh challenges and a 
chance to broaden the Judge’s experience.  
Those Street Judges identiﬁed as performing to the 
highest standard, and who have kept their noses clean 
in the eyes of the Special Judicial Squad (SJS), can then 
be elevated to the rank of Senior Street Judge. These 
individuals are not tied to a speciﬁc sector, instead they 
are allowed to roam the entire city intervening in 
incidents as they see ﬁt, or as directed by the Council of 
Five or the Chief Judge.  
A particularly eﬀective Senior Street Judge may be 
promoted to the important rank of Sector Chief. 
However, for many of Mega‐City One’s ﬁnest this can 
prove unpopular. Most Senior Street Judges prefer the 
direct approach, meting out justice on the streets 
where their experience and knowledge are most 
eﬀective in maintaining the rule of the law. 
Becoming a Judge is a lifelong undertaking and many 
individuals falter on the arduous path. Some end up on 
the streets disenchanted and bitter at what might have 
been. However, for many Street Judges, Resyk will be 
their ﬁnal destination.  
58

## PDF Page 61

CADETS  
Potential Judge candidates are usually inducted into 
the Academy of Law at the tender age of ﬁve. Here, 
they are subject to rigorous academic training in all 
aspects of the law that governs the North American 
Mega‐Cities. By the end of the process, they have 
acquired a level of knowledge that would exceed those 
of the most senior lawyers and justice oﬃcials of the 
early 21st century. 
In parallel to their academic training, they undergo an 
intensive physical and mental exercise programme that 
hones their ﬁrearms and close combat skills whilst also 
preparing them to endure the various injuries and 
physical hardships that life patrolling the streets will 
throw at them. 
Whilst the life of a cadet is mostly taken up by study 
and training, if a particularly serious situation or  
crisis demands the Justice Department deploy more 
manpower, cadets may be armed and sent out onto 
the streets, usually as part of a carefully monitored 
team engaging in fairly low intensity operations.  
One highlight of a cadet’s continual assessment is  
the ‘Hotdog Run’ where a group of cadets 
accompanied by Senior Judges are sent on patrol in 
the radioactive hell of the Cursed Earth beyond the 
Mega‐City’s walls. 
However, during the Necropolis and Chaos Day 
incidents cadets were deployed into highly dangerous 
situations normally only handled by a fully qualiﬁed 
Judge. Many cadets performed bravely and well, as 
beﬁtted their training, but the losses incurred had 
serious future implications for the Justice Department 
when it was already struggling to maintain enough 
qualiﬁed Street Judges.  
ROoKIES 
“Incorrect sentencing is an automatic fail. Disobeying a 
direct order from your assessment oﬃcer is an automatic 
fail. Losing your primary weapon or having it taken from 
you is an automatic fail.” 
At the age of 20 a Cadet Judge enters a period of duty 
where they come under the close supervision of more 
experienced Judges. They will regularly patrol the 
streets dispensing the Law as would their senior peers. 
During this period, they are known as Rookie Judges. 
They carry Lawgiver pistols and have virtually the same 
uniform as a Street Judge, diﬀering only in a distinctive 
white helmet and a half‐eagle variant of the standard 
Judge’s chest badge. Although this may mark them as 
being inexperienced, one can only pity the criminal that 
sees them as a soft touch. A trained rookie is more than 
capable of handling even a hardened street criminal.  
At the end of their probation, rookies must pass their 
Final Street Assessment. This takes the form of a single 
street patrol with a Senior Street Judge. At all times 
during the patrol the rookie must convince their 
assessor that they are worthy of the role. Should they 
be deemed to have committed any transgression in the 
eyes of the senior judge it is an ‘Automatic Fail’. There is 
no appeal and no second chance. The individual either 
immediately leaves the Justice Department or is found 
a menial labour role elsewhere in the organisation. 
JUDGES 
Whilst all Judges must undergo a Final Street 
Assessment only a select number will be immediately 
given street duties. The vast majority will be assigned 
auxiliary roles to ﬁll the huge staﬃng needs of the 
Justice Department. Whilst a Street Judge may be the 
spear tip of the department, he or she requires a huge 
amount of logistical and technical support to perform 
their jobs. Fully qualiﬁed Judges hold the vast majority 
of roles in the department. They guard the Iso‐cubes, 
man the sector control rooms, drive the support 
vehicles, as well as handling all administrative roles. It is 
important for the morale of the frontline Street Judges 
to know individuals who understand what it is to be a 
Judge are supporting them. Of course, at any given 
moment, all Judges are expected to don the helmet and 
go onto the streets if the situation demands it. Certainly, 
no other organisation has an accounting department 
whose members possess tactical and combat skills 
superior to those of a military special forces unit!  
DENIZENS OF  
MEGA-CITY ONE
59

## PDF Page 62

60
A Cadet and Rookie Psi‐Judge  
will go through the same regime of 
training as their ‘norm’ colleagues but they 
also receive special extra‐curricular psychic 
training under the tutelage of experienced members 
of Psi Division, who keep close watch for dangerous 
deviations or abnormalities. After graduation  
Psi‐Judges are attached to a sector house for up to four 
years, where they develop and mesh their street and  
Psi skills to the greater beneﬁt of the Department.  
However, many Psi‐Judges prove too mentally fragile 
for the rigours of street duty and are forced to serve  
in support roles such as Pre‐Cog and remote sensing, 
or in support of the Public Surveillance Unit (PSU). 
More stable members will perform their duties 
alongside their Street Division colleagues, bringing 
the Law to the Mega‐City in less than conventional 
ways. The SJS too have their own Psi‐Judges, a 
formidable tool in their quest to root out corruption 
and weakness amongst their Justice Department 
peers. As a matter of necessity, the Justice 
Department keeps a very close eye on these most 
powerful of individuals, as a rogue Psi‐Judge could 
very quickly become a deadly threat. 
JUDGE SPECIALIST: MED DIVISION 
Street Judges may be adept in ﬁrst aid but there are 
times when injuries can incapacitate even such a robust 
individual. In such circumstances it is the primary role of 
a Med Judge to provide paramedic aid to a Judge or 
assist civilians and perpetrators injured in Justice 
Department operations. Whilst many Med Judges 
operate the advanced medical facilities found in every 
Sector House, others perform their duties on the 
streets, either with special quick reaction ambulance 
units (‘meat wagons’) or sometimes as part of a street 
patrol operating in dangerous areas of the city where 
combat‐related injuries are more likely. 
SPECIALIST JUDGES 
Some Judges at a very early stage in their training 
display certain physical or mental traits that the 
department considers useful and wishes to exploit. 
These can vary from an aﬃnity with machines to an 
understanding of the workings of the human body, or 
more radically, the display of latent psionic talents. 
Such individuals are noted and given special training 
and access to advanced curricula.  
JUDGE SPECIALIST: PSI DIVISION 
“It’s gettin’ so I’m spendin’ more time in other people’s 
heads than I do in my own!” 
From its inception, the Justice Department has been 
keenly interested in the paranormal. By the middle of 
the 21st century it had been conclusively proven that 
psychic powers existed, allowing a tiny subset of the 
human population to predict the future, read minds, and 
to manipulate the physical environment around them. 
The beneﬁts of harnessing these powers in the 
enforcement of the law immediately attracted the 
attention of the ﬂedgling Justice Department. Psi 
Division was created to identify cadets displaying active 
or latent psychic talents and to mould them into an elite 
and extraordinary group of Judges capable of harnessing 
their powers in support of the Justice Department. 


---

## PDF Page 63

JUDGE SPECIALIST: TEK DIVISION 
Whilst the world of Mega‐City One may sometimes 
appear as an uncivilised environment, it is nevertheless 
built upon technology that would seem miraculous to an 
observer from the early 21st century. Routine starﬂight, 
anti‐grav, energy weapons, holograms and advanced 
robots are now an accepted part of everyday life. The 
Justice Department keeps a very close eye on new or 
alien technological developments, especially where they 
might aid or tempt a citizen to break the law. It is the 
responsibility of the Judges of Tek Division to police this 
new technology and to ensure that the Justice 
Department keeps pace with any advances. Most Tek 
Judges spend the ﬁrst four years after their graduation 
in Sector Houses maintaining the Justice Department’s 
vehicles, weapons and other equipment. However, when 
deemed necessary, they provide technical ﬁeld support 
for Street Judges in the areas of forensics, technological 
threats and advanced intrusion techniques. 
JUDGE SPECIALIST: SPECIAL JUDICIAL SQUAD (SJS) 
“Depending who you ask, they’re ‘the Judges who judge 
the Judges’, the brakes on fascism, or a bunch of drokking 
drokk‐headed evil drokks.” 
A Mega‐City One Judge is an extraordinarily powerful 
individual, empowered and encouraged to act as judge, 
jury, and executioner. However, with such great power 
comes an even greater responsibility to the law itself and 
the citizens it is meant to protect. Despite rigorous 
training and extensive screening some Judges make 
mistakes in dispensing the law, and others can fall into 
the error of believing themselves to be above it. Worst of 
all, the possibility of corruption always exists and nothing 
is more corrosive to the rule of law than a dirty Judge. 
This raises the question, who can judge the Judges?  
The answer is the Special Judicial Squad (SJS). This 
division is a shadowy branch of the Justice Department 
tasked with policing and investigating other Judges. 
Any Judge suspected of breaking the law is subject to 
intensive SJS scrutiny. If a crime is proven, the 
Judge can face a range of penalties from 
dismissal to the ultimate sanction of exile to 
the penal colony on Saturn’s largest moon, 
Titan. Here radical non‐reversible 
surgical alteration is required to survive 
the harsh Titanian environment.  
The SJS carry out regular internal 
Department Blitzes, investigating 
Judges, searching their records 
for any violations, focussing 
on personal accounts, 
arrest reports, and oﬀ‐
duty pursuits. These are 
supposed to be 
random, however, 
sometimes petty 
rivalries and 
grudges have aﬀected this – you may want to ask Joe 
Dredd about his experiences with the SJS. 
However, the SJS has its own problems, with many 
examples of its Judges and senior oﬃcials drunk on the 
power of the role, acting with unjustiﬁed zeal, and 
overstepping their boundaries. This was of course most 
obvious during the time of Cal, when the SJS became a 
terror squad enforcing the whims of this insane 
individual. Despite such a tortured past, the necessity 
of the SJS has never been in doubt. No one can think 
themselves above the Law, not even Judges. 
JUDGE SPECIALIST: THE WALlY SQUAD 
“Me? I’m nobody. A ghost. A hole in the crowd… a big, 
fat zero.” 
There are some criminal investigations that require 
intelligence gathering, where the presence of a 
uniformed Judge brandishing a Lawgiver is not 
appropriate. For these activities the Justice Department 
has formed an undercover division known informally in 
the Department as the ‘Wally Squad’. These Judges 
adopt civilian garb and move amongst the street life of 
Mega‐City One always on the lookout for leads on 
criminal activity. If required, they will inﬁltrate gangs 
and criminal organisations on highly dangerous 
undercover missions. Activities like these are abhorrent 
to most Street Judges who tend to view their Wally 
Squad colleagues as necessary ‘oddballs’. 
Whilst some undercover operations can be concluded in 
a short time, dedicated squad members have been 
known to spend years on a case, all the time 
maintaining a false identity. There is a danger in such 
deep cover that some Wally Squad members, such as 
the infamous Lenny Zero, have had their outlook and 
life values completely changed. Their beliefs have been 
sequestered by the roles they are playing, and many 
have ‘gone rogue’ working out of sight, but not out of 
mind, of the Justice Department. A Judge gone rogue 
can become the most dangerous of criminals.  
Denizens of mega-city one

WEAPONS  
A Judge’s primary personal weapons are their  
Lawgiver Mk2 Pistol, a Day Stick and, as a last resort, 
their Boot Knife. The Notoriety costs for Judges given 
above includes the cost of these weapons. Due to their 
training with weapon handling, Judges may be 
equipped with four hands of weapons. 
Judges of all ranks may also be equipped with the 
M2000 Widowmaker, Lawrod Mk5 or Stub Gun for an 
additional Notoriety cost: 
• M2000 Widowmaker . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . +6 Notoriety 
• Lawrod Mk5 . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . +5 Notoriety 
• Stub gun . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . +10 Notoriety 
RULES 
Gunﬁghter/Brawler 
Whilst some Street Judges excel with ﬁrearms, others 
prefer to mete out justice close and personal. See the 
core rules for the Brawler and Gunﬁghter skills. 
Fortitude 
The life of a Judge is extremely dangerous and the  
risk of being wounded is quite high. Nevertheless, 
Mega‐City One’s ﬁnest are expected to keep 
performing their role even if injured. A Judge with 
Fortitude will suﬀer no stat loss for one injury or stun 
result that they are suﬀering.  
Respect the Law  
The reputation of the Judges precedes them, and  
few perps are enthusiastic about tangling with the 
Justice Department. Judges are trained to always adopt 
a dominating posture and use their voice in a 
commanding manner. This, combined with the 
distinctive uniform, is often enough to intimidate a perp. 
Whenever a Judge activates, they can immediately 
attempt to intimidate all opposing models that are 
within line of sight and within 8" range of the Judge, 
without using an action. To determine the 
eﬀect of the intimidation, take a Cool test for 
the Judge. If the test is passed, for each 
[SPECIAL] result rolled, one model within range 
is pinned. 
Let’s Ride!  
There are few more imposing 
images of the Justice Department 
than a Judge patrolling the Big 
Meg on their Lawmaster bike. 
Judges display riding skills even 
surpassing those of the famed 
bikers of the Mega‐City 5000 
races. A Judge riding a 
Lawmaster is not affected by the 
Passengers Stunned result.

A.I., 2 Passengers, Vehicle, Wilko Judge
One of the greatest assets at a Judge’s disposal is the 
Lawmaster bike. Over the years, many iterations of this 
formidable vehicle have been issued by the Justice 
Department and each has served alongside the Judges 
on the front line against crime and  performed with 
distinction. There are few more iconic sights on the 
streets of Mega‐City One than a stoic Judge astride  
a Lawmaster 
The Lawmaster is a powerful and highly manoeuvrable 
motorbike providing Judges with a fast and eﬃcient 
mode of transport in Mega‐City One, whilst also being a 
stockpile of essential equipment and ammunition for the 
Judge. Powered by a 4,000cc engine and heavily 
armoured, it also boasts considerable oﬀensive ﬁrepower 
with twin autocannons and in some variants a heavy 
vehicle‐stopping laser. One of its most interesting facets 
is the A.I. computer that allows the bike limited 
autonomy and the ability to follow basic orders from the 
Judge. It is not uncommon for a Judge to park up his bike 
giving it orders to ﬁre on a particular location whilst the 
dismounted Judge moves in from a ﬂank.   
SPECIAL RULES 
A.I.  
The Lawmaster is a fully A.I. controlled vehicle and 
can operate entirely independently. Unlike most 
vehicles, the Lawmaster generates its own Action 
chip just like any other model. However, despite its 
Cool of 4, it only ever generates a normal Action chip 
and not a Star chip. 
2 Passengers  
Only Judges and SJS Judges of all ranks and specialities, 
including the Mechanismo Mk1 Auxiliary Judges, may 
ride as passengers on a Lawmaster bike. 
Vehicle 
The Justice Department’s Lawmaster bike is no simple 
mount – it is such a capable, self‐driving and heavily 
armed machine that it is actually treated as a separate 
model in its own right and follows all the rules for 
vehicles as detailed beginning on page 30.

Wilko Judge  
Only a Judge (of any rank or speciality), Mechanismo 
Mk1 Auxiliary Judge or SJS Judge model may board a 
Lawmaster.  An opponent cannot use the ‘Knock Knock’ 
rule to board a Lawmaster. Additionally, when selecting 
models for your faction you cannot select a Lawmaster 
unless you also have a Judge to ride it.

have mounted diﬀerent weapons in order to assist the

the Cyclops Laser and the Bike Cannons, and the Mk3

## PDF Page 68

WEAPONS OF THE JUSTICE DEPARTMENT 
The Colt Firearms Lawgiver Mk2 has been the standard 
sidearm for the Justice Department for well over 50 years. 
The advanced Mk2 variant has more recently superseded 
the original Mk1, retaining and improving on many of the 
original’s standout features.  
Early in the history of the Justice Department it was clear 
that Judges would need to deal with a wide variety of 
combat and law enforcing actions, ranging from simple 
arrests to full‐blown quasi‐military engagements with 
well‐armed hostile forces. Rather than burdening Judges 
with multiple weapon systems, the ﬁrst Chief Judge, 
Eustace Fargo, initiated the Lawgiver program. This 
sought to miniaturise a wide variety of ordnance to the 
point where they could be ﬁred from a single pistol‐sized 
weapon. The introduction of a small computer core and 
voice recognition software in later versions made 
selecting the right round for the job relatively easy.  
A few early incidents of criminals taking Lawgivers from 
fallen Judges forced the addition of the handprint failsafe 
sensor. When a Judge is assigned a Lawgiver in default 
mode, it samples their unique 
DNA via the pistol grip and from 
that point on, only that Judge 
can ﬁre the weapon. If the 
weapon is picked up by another 
person, even another Judge, 
and an attempt is made to ﬁre 
it, the Lawgiver immediately 
detonates its power cell 
creating an explosion powerful 
enough to blow a hand oﬀ. 
A Lawgiver’s ammo loadout is 
primarily a mix of Execution 
and Armour Piercing bullets. A 
smaller number of specialist 
rounds may also be carried but 
a Judge’s supply will vary 
depending on their activities 
and how recently they have 
been able to resupply at a 
Sector House armoury. 
The stats above are for a 
Lawgiver Mk2 ﬁring  
standard Execution rounds, 
with diﬀerent options to use 
Stun Shots or Armour  
Piercing rounds. Other  
special rounds may only be 
ﬁred by playing the 
appropriate Armoury cards. 
Armour Piercing 
The Lawgiver can use Armour 
Piercing (AP) rounds instead of 
standard Execution rounds on 
a shot at the option of the 
owning player. AP rounds 
reduce the target’s Resist by 1 
against hits but impose a ‐1 
Shoot modiﬁer on the attack 
due to recoil.

Special Rounds 
The use of special rounds requires the expenditure of 
speciﬁc Armoury cards: 
• In all cases, a Special Round is ﬁred using the short 
and long range Shoot modiﬁers of the Lawgiver Mk2.  
• If a Hot Shot, Hi‐Ex, Ricochet or Stumm Gas round 
misses, then follow the Blast weapon scatter rules 
on page 48.  
• Special Rounds always require an Aimed Fire double‐
action and do not get additional Aimed Fire beneﬁts. 
• Ammo pouches: Normally when an Armoury card 
is expended it is discarded. However, if the card 
has been used for a Lawgiver shot there is a 
chance that the card is not immediately 
discarded. After each Special Rounds shot 
roll a Cool test for the ﬁring model. If any 
result is a [SPECIAL] then the card is retained 
and may be used again. The card can 
potentially be used multiple times in a game 
turn, as long as it keeps getting retained.

LAWGIVER AMmUNITION 
Standard Execution Round. This is a highly 
eﬀective bullet with high stopping power and is the 
standard default ammunition for the Lawgiver. 
Armour Piercing. A Depleted Uranium bullet 
jacketed in Teﬂon makes this round capable of 
penetrating most personal body armour, robot 
chassis’, or protective cover. 
Ricochet (‘Dodgems’). Designed by the same 
corporation responsible for BOING® the Ricochet 
round is ﬁred at a relatively low velocity, and rather 
than breaking up or penetrating when it hits a 
barrier it will instead bounce oﬀ at an angle 
potentially hitting multiple targets. It is highly 
eﬀective when ﬁred into a small room or vehicle.  
Hi‐Ex. A round packed with a highly volatile 
explosive compound that explodes on impact with 
the power of a frag hand bomb. This is generally 
used against vehicles and robots.  
Incendiary (‘Hotshot’). Using a Phosphex 
compound this round sets ﬁre to the target it hits, 
burning with an incandescent heat that even water 
and dedicated ﬁre extinguishers cannot quell. 
Heat Seeker. By far the most sophisticated 
Lawgiver round. This is a miniaturised heat‐
seeking missile that can home in on the body 
heat of humans and sufficiently similar aliens. 
Before firing the Judge points the Lawgiver in the 
general direction of the target allowing the round 
to ‘see’ the target’s infra‐red signature. An 
audible beep indicates a lock and the trigger can 
be pulled. Whilst the Heat Seeker is a highly 
effective weapon, it must be used with caution as 
other human heat sources in close proximity to 
the target can attract the bullet. Death of 
innocents from incorrectly used heat seeker 
rounds has seen a number of Judges sentenced to 
the Titan penal colony.  
Stumm Gas Pellet. Sophisticated miniaturisation 
techniques allow a Lawgiver Stumm Gas pellet to 
have the eﬀectiveness of a regular grenade.

Introduced in 2214 to replace the Lawrod riﬂe, the M2000 
Widowmaker is speciﬁcally designed to give Judges 
eﬀective ﬁrepower when dealing with large hostile crowds 
or mobs. Essentially, it combines a rapid ﬁring spit gun 
chambered for modiﬁed Executioner rounds and a pump 
action launcher capable of ﬁring standard Frag and 
Stumm Gas grenades.  
Special Rounds 
Each time a model using a Widowmaker activates it has 
the option to use either standard Execution rounds 
(using the basic stats shown above), Stumm Gas, or 
Frag. Stumm Gas and Frag require an Aimed Fire 
double‐action to use.

MELEe WEAPONS 
From a very early stage in their training 
Judges are encouraged to only use lethal 
force as a last resort. Lawbreakers ordinarily 
should be neutralised and arrested using a 
combination of authoritative vocal 
intimidation backed up by the threat of non‐
lethal, but painful, violence. This has meant 
that for the vast majority of low threat perps, 
an intimate conversation with a Judge’s Day 
Stick is the usual result of an  
encounter with the ‘Jays’.
Realising that Judges required a long‐range alternative to 
the Lawgiver pistol, the long‐barrelled Lawrod was 
developed using the Lawgiver Mk1 as its base. The 
Lawrod will only ﬁre standard Execution rounds but its 
advanced sights allow it to be accurate over a long range 
and, despite being replaced in general service by the 
Widowmaker in 2114, the Mk5 version is still the preferred 
weapon of Justice Department snipers.

Denizens of mega-city one
JIMPS 
These are Judge impersonators 
wearing close facsimiles of the 
Judge uniform. Whether they are 
just fakers attempting to pass 
themselves off as the Mega-
City’s finest, deluded crazies or 
simply attendees of a fancy-
dress party, these individuals are 
taking a great risk with their 
lives. A citizen spotted in a fake 
uniform will be subject to 
immediate arrest and can be 
sentenced for up to twenty years 
in the cubes.  
It is also a sad fact of life that if the Jays don’t arrest them then a gun-happy futsie or

After the war most surviving stub guns were collected, 
deactivated and destroyed. However, rumours persist 
that some remain unaccounted for and it is a Justice 
Department nightmare that they have fallen into the 
hands of criminal elements.  
Stub Gun Special Rules 
• Stub guns negate any positive Resist modifiers  
for cover. 
• Every time a stub gun hits a target 
and the 8 Power dice are rolled, if  
2 x [SPECIAL] results are rolled the 
stub gun explodes. Calculate a Blast 
(6"), Power 8 effect centred on the firing model. 
Even if the weapon explodes, apply 
damage to the target 
as normal.
The Stub Gun is perhaps the ultimate expression of the 
hand‐held directed energy weapon. Developed during 
the latter days of the Atomic Wars, the stub gun is 
rumoured to use technology recovered from a crashed 
alien spacecraft. Using a barely controlled fusion 
reaction the stub gun can fire an immensely powerful 
beam of coherent energy capable of cutting through 
almost any known material.  
However, with such power comes a fundamental problem. 
The fusion reaction can become unstable, exceeding the 
stub gun’s ability to contain it and causing the weapon to 
explode. Stub gun overloads usually kill the gunner and 
anyone unlucky enough to be close by. Initially 
considered too dangerous to use, the Justice Department 
sealed away the initial cache of prototypes. 
They did see action during the desperate times of 
the Apocalypse War when they were distributed to 
Judges in the so‐called Apocalypse Squads 
struggling to staunch East‐Meg Rad‐Sweeper 
assaults. On numerous occasions the stub guns 
killed enemy tanks and sliced Sov Strato V assault 
ships clean in half! However, many Judges died as their 
stub guns overloaded.

A Street Judge, Judge or a Rookie Judge may be 
upgraded to the Psi‐Judge Specialisation by giving 
them one of the following Psi Skills.  
Psionic Control  
The trained mind of an experienced psyker is quite 
capable of inﬂuencing an unsuspecting opponent. The 
Psi‐Judge can send a powerful suggestion to the target 
causing them to take an involuntary action. This can be 
as simple as urging them to move out of cover or 
perform more complex tasks such as opening a door or 
even snap ﬁring a weapon. So powerful are these 
suggestions that even if the willpower of the target is 
strong enough to oppose the intrusion the shock will 
temporarily stop them in their tracks.  
Psionic Control requires a double‐action to use.  
The Psi‐Judge using it must be within 12" of the target. 
Psionic Control does not aﬀect robots and A.I. 
controlled vehicles.
If the Psi‐Judge has line of sight to the target, the 
opposing model must take a Cool test. If it fails, the 
Justice Department player may make the target model 
take a single action, but not a double‐action.  
If the Psi‐Judge does not have line of sight to the target, 
the opposing model may roll one extra Combat dice for 
their Cool test. In either case if the target passes the 
Cool test the model is pinned instead.  
The target model may not be forced to take any action 
that would catastrophically harm it such as jumping oﬀ a 
building or eating a frag bomb. However, a victim could 
be forced to Snapshot at or Fight with a friendly model.  
Pre-Cog  
One of the most common powers encountered amongst 
psychics is that of pre‐cognition. This is the ability to see 
future events before they happen, allowing individuals to 
change or take a course of action to avoid some future 
mishap. On the streets, a trained Psi‐Judge with Pre‐Cog 
skills can try and predict the actions of a perp. By 
concentrating on the events of the next 30 seconds, a Psi‐
Judge can react to, or alert a colleague of, an impending 
threat; shouting a warning before the assailant has pulled 
a trigger or struck with a blade.  
To use Pre‐Cog a Psi‐Judge must be activated 
and perform an Overwatch action following 
all the normal rules for this double‐action. As 
a Psi‐Judge on Overwatch is predicting the 
short‐term future, they are less surprised by 
the actions of enemy models. A Psi‐Judge 
can, therefore, take an Aimed Shot even if 
the model they are reacting to performs a 
Move, Sprint or Charge action.  
Alternatively, if any Judge within line of 
sight of the Psi‐Judge using Pre‐Cog 
is subject to a ranged or close 
combat attack they may re‐roll 
their Evade dice when dodging 
thanks to the Pre‐Cog’s 
warning. The Psi‐Judge’s 
Overwatch token is 
immediately removed 
after this re‐roll.  
70

## PDF Page 73

Mind Probe!  
A trained psionic can project a psychic probe 
directly into the brain of another person. This 
sudden shocking intrusion of an outsider’s 
thoughts is usually enough to create a paralysing 
panic which overwhelms the target. 
Upon activating, a Psi‐Judge can manifest this power 
as a double‐action. The Psi‐Judge using it must be 
within 12" of the target model. Mind Probe does not 
aﬀect robots and A.I. controlled vehicles. 
Make a Cool test for the Psi‐Judge 
counting [SPECIAL] and [HIT] results as 
successes. Each success rolled 
automatically inﬂicts one Stun marker on the target. 
Roll a Cool test for the target model, adding 
one Combat dice if the Psi‐Judge does not have 
line of sight to it. Each [ARMOUR] rolled negates 
one of the Stun results. Mind Probe can subdue 
a model if the number of stuns remaining 
equals or beats their Cool stat.  
MED JUDGE. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . +3 NOTORIETY 
A Judge or Rookie Judge can be upgraded to a Med 
Judge specialisation. This gives the model the Combat 
Medic skill. 
Combat Medic 
Four years of intensive training attached to a Sector 
House Hospital, along with the practical experience of 
numerous patrols accompanying Street Judges, hones 
a Med Judge into an extremely eﬀective combat 
medic. Equipped with Med‐Packs and advanced 
diagnostic tools, a Med Judge is very often the 
diﬀerence between life and death for a seriously 
wounded Judge or citizen.  
A Med Judge can use the Hunker Down double‐action 
to attempt to remove Injury or Stun markers from a 
single friendly model within 1", even if that model is 
incapacitated. Roll a Resist test using the 
injured model’s starting Resist stat. For each 
[Armour] result one Injury or Stun marker may 
be removed.  This skill can be used to revive 
incapacitated and subdued models. See page 
19 for the Hunker Down action.  
TEK JUDGE . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . +4 NOTORIETY 
All Tek Judges are trained to maintain and repair their 
Sector House’s extensive inventory of weapons, 
vehicles and equipment. An in‐depth knowledge of 
mechanics and electronic systems provides a Tek Judge 
with all the skills needed to operate specialist detection 
and forensic analysis equipment in the ﬁeld as well as 
supplying direct support for other Judges. 
A Judge or Rookie Judge can be upgraded to a Tek Judge 
specialisation. This gives the model the Armourer skill. 
71
Armourer Skill 
Before the beginning 
of the game draw one additional 
Armoury card for each Tek Judge present 
in the player’s faction. The player is then 
allowed to keep one more Armoury card than usual. 
For example, the Justice Department player has 
included two Tek Judges in their force. This means 
they draw eight Armoury cards at the beginning of 
the game (two extra for the two Teks on top of the 
usual six) and keep four of them (one more than 
usual because a Tek Judge is present). 
SJS JUDGE . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . FREe 
A Street Judge may be given the SJS specialisation for 
free. They may be armed with any weapon. A Street 
Judge can replace the standard Respect for the Law 
with the One‐way trip to Titan. 
One-way Trip to Titan  
Throughout a Judge’s training they are psychologically 
conditioned to remain in awe of any Judge wearing the 
skull‐emblazoned uniform of the SJS. A Judge gone 
rogue is an extremely dangerous person. Therefore, 
those who judge the Judges need all the help they can 
get when taking on such individuals. 
Any Justice Department model is subject to the Respect 
the Law rule when facing SJS Judges. The mere sight of 
a distinctively uniformed SJS Judge can compel even 
experienced Mega‐City One Judges to freeze with 
indecision. When SJS Judges activate they can 
intimidate any Justice Department model within 12" 
and within line of sight for no cost. To determine the 
eﬀect of the intimidation, take a Cool test for the target 
model. If the test is failed, the target is pinned.

## PDF Page 74

HEROES AND VILlAINS  
OF THE JUSTICE DEPARTMENT  
 
JUDGE DREDd  
“I am the Law.” 
So says Judge Joseph Dredd, and it is no idle boast.  
For millions of Mega‐City One’s citizens Dredd is the 
living embodiment of stony‐faced judgement. Of the 
thousands of Judges that the Justice Department have 
placed on the streets of Mega‐City One there can be no 
greater symbol of the Law than Judge Dredd, not even 
the statue of Justice itself. The mention of Dredd’s 
name alone is enough to give even the most hardened 
criminals pause.  
Along with his brother Rico, Dredd was allegedly cloned 
from the DNA of the original Chief Judge, Eustace 
Fargo, back in 2066. Their creator, geneticist Morton 
Judd, gave the twins the intimidating surname of Dredd. 
Force‐grown in incubators to a physiological age of ﬁve 
the twins were indoctrinated with all the knowledge 
needed to be a dedicated and loyal servant of justice.  
Drafted as cadets in 2070, during the horrors of the 
Atomic War, they took part in the ﬁnal battles against 
the last president of the USA, Robert L. Booth. 
Graduating as a Street Judge in 2079, Dredd was 
immediately fully committed to the rule of law and 
relentlessly hunting lawbreakers in 
the Big Meg. He had no close friends and rarely 
fraternised with his colleagues, who christened him ‘Old 
Stony Face’. Even family and fraternal ties have proven 
secondary to the Law, as was made evident when Dredd 
turned his own clone‐brother over to the Justice 
Department after Rico had fallen victim to the dark side 
of a Judge’s absolute power over life and death. 
Dredd quickly rose to the rank of Senior Judge and has 
kept that role throughout his career, despite being ﬁrst 
oﬀered the job of Chief Judge in 2101 and many more 
times since. He has been appointed a temporary 
member of the Council of Five on several occasions. 
However, he is only truly content when patrolling the 
streets and dispensing the law directly. His well‐known 
dislike of bureaucracy keeps him well away from the 
Hall of Justice.  
During the Apocalypse War of 2104, after Mega‐City 
One had sustained hundreds of millions of casualties, 
Dredd carried out his most extreme application of 
Judgement to date. Leading a commando team into 
the wastelands around East‐Meg One he seized and 
used a Sov TAD (Total Annihilation Device) to obliterate 
the enemy Mega‐City, killing half a billion largely 
innocent civilians to end the war. 
If it is true that a man can be measured by the quality of 
his enemies then Dredd will take some beating. Over 
the years, he has clashed with mad Chief Judges, 
trans‐dimensional denizens of Dead World, 
super agents of the Sov Bloc, necromancers 
and powerful mutant pre‐cogs. Yet it’s 
probably safe to say that from Dredd’s 
perspective the bringing of one of 
these individuals to justice is no 
more important than the 
prosecution of a citizen 
littering or a Juve spraying 
graﬃti on a wall. Everyone is 
equal in the eyes of the law!  
Now over 70 years of age, and 
still active as a street Judge, 
who can say if Dredd’s 
constant crusade against 
lawbreakers in the Big Meg 
will ever end? 
72

SPECIAL RULES 
Drokk Me, it’s Dredd!!! 
The sight of Judge Dredd, or even the sound of his 
voice, will compel even hardened perps to consider the 
consequences of their actions. Whenever Dredd 
activates, he can immediately attempt to intimidate all 
opposing models that are within 12", without using an 
action. No characters are immune to this eﬀect. 
To determine the eﬀect of the intimidation, take a Cool 
test for each model. If the test is failed the target model 
is pinned. 
Gaze into the Fist of Dredd! 
Dredd has few peers when it comes to hand‐to‐hand 
combat, breaking the skulls of lawbreakers who may be 
a little reluctant to come quietly. Judge Dredd is subject 
to the Brawler rules that can be found on page 26.  
Gunﬁghter  
Dredd’s cadet record for live‐ﬁre combat shooting 
remains the gold standard at the Academy. See Page 22 
for Gunﬁghter rules. 
Incredible shot  
Years of experience with ﬁrearms on the streets have 
honed Dredd’s marksmanship to almost unbelievable 
levels of accuracy. Even when ﬁring from the hip he 
rarely misses. In addition to beneﬁtting from the 
Gunﬁghter skill, Judge Dredd always classes the Aimed 
Fire action as a single action instead of a double‐action. 
However, he may not perform two Aimed Fire actions in 
a single activation. The second ranged ﬁre action must 
be a Snapshot. This skill allows Dredd to shoot an 
Aimed Fire only weapon (Lawgiver with special ammo 
being a premium choice) and perform another single 
action in the same activation.  
Indestructible 
“The pain must be excruciating — he shouldn’t even be 
able to stand, let alone walk!”  
During his career protecting Mega‐City One Dredd has 
been shot, stabbed, burned, tortured, poisoned, and 
physically abused thousands of times over yet, Old 
Stony Face always recovers. Whether this is a result of 
Dredd’s clone origins or just simple superhuman 
endurance is not certain.  
Judge Dredd suﬀers no stat losses for injury or stun 
results that he suﬀers. The only way you put him down 
is to incapacitate or subdue him.  
Let’s Ride!  
When Dredd is a passenger on a Lawmaster he is not 
aﬀected by the Passengers Stunned result.  
WEAPONS AND EQUIPMENT 
Dredd’s primary personal weapons are his Lawgiver 
Mk2 Pistol, a Day Stick and Boot Knife.  
Judge Dredd may also be  equipped with a M2000 
Widowmakeror the Lawrod Mk5 for an additional 
Notoriety cost.  
• M2000 Widowmaker . . . . . . . . . . . . . . +6 Notoriety  
• Lawrod MK5 . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . +5 Notoriety

## PDF Page 78

PSI-JUDGE CASsANDRA ANDERSON 
“Anderson can’t resist her little jokes. Not normal for a 
Judge! Still, Psi’s are highly‐strung. We’ve got to allow 
them their little personality defects.” 
One of Psi Division’s greatest stars is Cassandra 
Anderson. She specialises in the ﬁelds of telepathy and 
pre‐cognition, where she has few peers. Born in 2080 
Anderson is probably as far removed from Dredd’s ideal 
of what a Judge should be as can be imagined. She is an 
outspoken critic of many aspects of the Justice 
Department, displays her emotions, and has empathy 
for others. Particularly irritating to Dredd and the 
Justice Department’s rather strait‐laced leadership is 
her ﬂippant, joking demeanour and ability to interact 
with other Judges on an informal level. Despite all that 
when the safety of Mega‐City One is threatened she is 
uncompromising and prepared to use both lethal 
psychic and physical force against any threats. This 
aspect of Anderson is 
respected by Dredd, and 
they have teamed up on 
numerous occasions, 
including the desperate 
inﬁltration mission that led to the destruction of East‐
Meg One. 
It is Anderson’s experiences with the Dark Judges of 
Dead World which has aﬀected her more than any of 
her other cases, especially as she has been psychicly 
possessed by Judge Death and escaped his necrotic 
clutches on more than one occasion.  
In their many attempts to bring the sentence of death 
to Mega‐City One, the Dark Judges have been attracted 
to Anderson like moths to a ﬂame, despite her being 
instrumental in their defeat many times. For her part, 
Anderson harbours a deep hatred for these beings that 
represent the opposite of all she holds sacred.  
Anderson has been censured and disciplined by Justice 
Department many times for her unconventional 
procedures and has abandoned her duty several times 
in protest, only to always return. However, like Dredd, 
she is a venerated ﬁgure, viewed by rank and ﬁle Judges 
with a sense of awe and, like him, she will most likely 
serve until her body and mind can no longer perform 
the role of Judge.

## PDF Page 80

SJS SENIOR JUDGE ALEX GERHART 
“I’m the one who’s going to take you down. I’m going to 
ﬁnally give you what you deserve. And you know it!”  
Now more than 75% Cyborg, Alex Gerhart was an  
up‐and‐coming star in the sinister ranks of the  
Special Judicial Squad. Unfortunately, like so many  
of his colleagues, he had an unhealthy obsession  
with Judge Dredd. Not only had the venerable Judge 
been instrumental in exposing the worst excesses of 
the SJS under Judge Cal, he also represented the one 
thing that the SJS could simply not process: an 
incorruptible Judge.  
For several years, Gerhart pursued a vendetta against 
Dredd to uncover some transgression, no matter how 
small. While investigating the death of a Tek Judge,  
he unsuccessfully tried to force Dredd to admit having 
a guilty conscience about the 350 million citizens  
who fell to the Chaos Virus, a bio‐weapon unleashed 
by Sov agents in revenge for Dredd destroying East‐
Meg One.  
Ultimately Gerhart’s obsession with Dredd would cost 
him dearly. He saved Dredd’s life by taking the impact 
of a missile meant for his nemesis. He simply could not 
let Dredd die before guilt was fully proven. Gerhart’s 
self‐sacriﬁce saw fully half of his shattered body being 
replaced by cybernetic parts. 
Strangely, it was Gerhart’s obsession that led him to be 
chosen by Dredd to join a team sent to Titan to 
investigate the penal colony’s silence. Both men 
suﬀered greatly on the mission and Gerhart ﬁnally 
concluded that Dredd was exactly what he appeared to 
be, incorruptible. In the fallout from the mission and the 
resulting return of the bizarrely altered inmates from 
Enceladus, Gerhart was wounded even more severely 
by ex‐Judge Amie Nixon, leaving him with only a 
quarter of his body still organic. 
Unfortunately, this new mutual trust between Gerhart 
and Dredd has brought him to the attention of the 
psychopathic SJS Judge Pin, a covert multiple serial 
killer of Judges who Pin found wanting. Accused of 
being unﬁt for SJS duty, Gerhart took the Long Walk 
into the Cursed Earth. Whilst Pin might believe Gerhart 
has been added to her grim tally, Gerhart is nothing if 
not a survivor. Only time will tell.  
78

## PDF Page 82

MECHANISMO MK1 AUXILIARY JUDGE 
“Shut them down! Shut them all down!” 
Robots see wide use in Mega‐City One and have made 
most of the human population permanently 
unemployed. However, traumatic experiences with 
battle droids during the conﬂict against President Booth, 
and the horrifying events of the 2099 Robot Wars, means 
the arming of such machines is highly controversial, 
heavily regulated and in most cases illegal.  
However, following the catastrophic impacts of 
Necropolis and Sabbat’s ‘Day of Judgement’, Justice 
Department casualties have been so great that 
members of the Council of Five, including Chief Judge 
McGruder, believed that the Department was on the 
verge of losing control of the City. Many of the 
casualties had been amongst the Cadets, so fewer 
trained Judges were available to make up for critical 
losses. Despite some misgivings, the Chief Judge 
directed Tek Division to create robot Judge/auxiliaries 
with full judicial authority to arrest, sentence and, if 
necessary, execute criminals. Programming was based 
on Judge Dredd’s ‘Comportment’, his unoﬃcial guide to 
judgement and dispensing the law, and the ﬁrst of the 
so‐called Mechanismo Robots were deployed in 2114.  
Dredd, like many, was appalled that machines would be 
given the power of deciding on the life or death of a 
human. His objections were overridden, and initial 
reports were favourable. However, somewhat 
predictably, the new ‘Machine Law’ was soon 
compromised. Internal malfunctions due to 
overheating caused two of the robots to go wild, killing 
large numbers of innocent civilians and ignoring their 
shutdown orders. It took the combined ﬁrepower of a 
Pat Wagon and Judge Dredd himself to bring the rogue 
Mechanismo units down. Later marks of the 
Mechanismo have proved more reliable, but they are 
still viewed with extreme suspicion and their 
deployment remains highly controversial.

SPECIAL RULES 
Robot 
For all their sophistication Mechanismo units are still 
robots and so they only generate normal Action chips 
instead of Star chips, despite being Cool 4. They are also 
unaﬀected by Psi powers. 
Respect the Machine Law  
The mere sight of a hulking Mechanismo Mk1 Judge 
Auxiliary will give the most conﬁdent perp second 
thoughts before taking one on.  
Whenever a Mechanismo Mk1 activates they can 
immediately attempt to intimidate all opposing 
models that are within line of sight and within 8" 
range of the Mechanismo, without using an action. 
 To determine the effect of the intimidation, take a 
Cool test for the Mechanismo. If the test is 
passed, one model within range is pinned for 
each [SPECIAL] result rolled. 
Self-Repair Sub-Routines 
A Mechanismo is capable of rapid and highly 
eﬀective self‐repairs. If it is injured and uses the 
Hunker Down double‐action, it can potentially 
remove all Injury markers if enough successes  
are rolled. 
Shut Down Number 5! 
Overheating issues in the Mk1 Mechanismo  
units caused the circuits governing the 
identification and sentencing of criminals to fail, 
resulting in the deaths of numerous civilians and 
Justice Department personnel. 
A Mechanismo Mk1 may fail any time that it is 
activated using a Star chip or uses a double‐action. 
Make a Cool test for the Mechanismo at the end  
of its activation if either of the above conditions  
is met.  
If the Cool test is failed the opposing player may 
immediately activate the Mechanismo Mk1 and 
perform up to one single action or one double‐action 
with it, including shooting or close combat, against 
any model on the table, within in its line of sight, and 
within range of its weapons. After this activation is 
complete, the Mechanismo Mk1 is stunned and 
control returns to its original owner.

JUDGE DEATH 
“The crime is life; the sentence is death!” 
The being known as Judge Death came from an alternate 
universe classiﬁed in Justice Department records simply 
as Deadworld. Painstaking reconstruction of the scanty 
evidence available, psychic readings, and the words of 
Judge Death himself have revealed that he was once 
Sidney De’Ath, a sadist who got his kicks from inﬂicting 
pain and made his ﬁrst kills whist still at school. Sidney 
joined Deadworld’s version of the Justice Department 
simply to experience the pleasure of killing legally.  
He picked up the nickname ‘Judge Death’ due to his 
predilection for executing perps. A liaison with the 
necromantic witches Phobia and Nausea, the ‘Sisters of 
Death’ brought De’Ath to the belief that total justice 
could only be obtained if there were no lawbreakers, 
and this could only be achieved by the complete 
extermination of all life. Through the blackest magic, 
Sidney De’Ath was voluntarily transformed into the 
undead revenant now known as Judge Death. With the 
help of the sisters, and other like‐minded individuals, all 
life was wiped out on Deadworld.

WEAPONS 
However, Death was not satisﬁed as the witches had 
revealed to him the existence of alternate dimensions 
and a multitude of other worlds teeming with life. 
Using arcane technology to cross the dimensions, 
Judge Death arrived in Mega‐City One in 2102. 
Death’s physical form was destroyed after his ﬁrst 
rampage, but his spirit survived, trapped inside the 
body of Judge Anderson. He was freed by three 
other Dark Judges from Deadworld – Fear, Fire and 
Mortis – and together they murdered thousands of 
citizens before being repelled by Judges Dredd and 
Anderson. Thus began a constant cycle of attacks on 
the living that has plagued the Mega‐City ever since. 
Although Dredd and Anderson have stopped each 
attack, the combined death toll is in the millions. 
The problem facing Dredd and Anderson is that 
you cannot kill what does not live. Judge Death’s 
physical form is an animated corpse and is 
extremely diﬃcult to injure or destroy. Even If the 
host body is totally destroyed, Death’s 
spirit can simply leave the body and 
possess another living human.  
Death displays superhuman strength, and is able to  
lift and throw heavy objects or smash through obstacles. 
However, his favoured method of delivering justice is to 
use his ability to phase through matter to push his bony 
ﬁngers into the body of the victim, squeezing their heart 
to a pulp.

## PDF Page 86

JUDGE GRICE – INMATE JUDGE 
Despite rigorous psycho screening during their 
development and training it is inevitable that some 
Judges go bad. Usually their transgressions are 
detected and exposed by the SJS. Punishment, as 
would be expected, is severe and for the most serious 
crimes, exile to the penal colony on Titan is usual. 
Judge Grice was a well‐regarded member of the Justice 
Department and achieved the rank of Senior Judge. 
However, he fell under the control of the Dark Judges, like 
many of Mega‐City One’s ﬁnest, during the Necropolis 
incident, and that traumatic event had a signiﬁcant eﬀect 
on him. The growing democracy movement was fuelled 
by public belief that the Justice Department had failed 
during Necropolis, and this rankled deeply with Grice. 
Along with several co‐conspirators, he attempted to 
undermine Chief Judge McGruder and assassinate Dredd 
in the hope of preventing the People’s Vote. Dredd 
survived the attempt on his life, the coup failed and Grice 
and his co‐conspirators were sentenced to twenty years 
of hard labour on Titan.  
Titan, the largest moon of Saturn, has been 
terraformed to some extent but the methane 
atmosphere is still un‐breathable to a normal human. 
Inmates on Titan are therefore subjected to disﬁguring 
facial surgery that allows them to survive outside the 
prison complex. Escape is almost impossible with all 
shuttles being closely monitored and secure. Though 
breakouts and insurrections on Titan are rare they do 
occur, and the so‐called ‘Doomsday’ aﬀair was one of 
the most signiﬁcant.  
Against the odds, Grice managed to escape Titan at the 
head of a mass breakout of ex‐Judges. Fixated on 
revenge, Grice returned directly to Mega‐City One, 
crashing his stolen shuttles into major buildings and 
unleashing a deadly virus on the citizenry. The 
weakened Justice Department was initially unable to 
stop a violent takeover, however Grice’s increasingly 
unstable nature allowed Dredd to lead a counterattack. 
In the ﬁnal confrontation Dredd crushed and killed the 
ex‐Judge under the wheels of his Lawmaster Bike.

## PDF Page 88

KARL RAIDER – VIGILANTE EX-JUDGE 
Karl Raider became a Judge in 2079 and was a 
contemporary of Joe and Rico Dredd, whom he almost 
matched in marksmanship during training. However, in 
2099, Raider became romantically involved with a 
citizen. Such relationships are not tolerated by the 
Justice Department and he had no alternative but to 
resign his position in order to lead a normal life with 
her. Tragedy struck during the Apocalypse War when his 
partner was fatally irradiated by a nearby Sov nuclear 
detonation. Unable to stand the sight of his wife 
suﬀering the agonies of radiation sickness Raider 
performed a mercy killing, permanently traumatising 
the former Judge. 
Raider was unable to return to any form of normal life in 
the aftermath of the Apocalypse War. He became a 
vigilante, targeting those who took advantage of 
ordinary citizens like his lost love. Raider’s favoured 
weapons of choice were twin Sledgehammer heavy 
pistols ﬁtted with red‐dot sights. 
For many years, Raider stayed under the Justice 
Department’s radar until he cold bloodedly mutilated 
Murk Derryson, the head of a particularly vicious 
protection racket, and murdered twelve of his henchmen. 
Dredd quickly identiﬁed who the vigilante was and 
endeavoured to capture Raider alive, using Wally Squad 
Judge Lola Palmtree as bait. In the interim Derryson 
organised a Chump Dumping operation to lure Raider 
into a deadly trap. However, Dredd arrested Derryson, 
who died resisting, but then had to face Raider and 
attempt to arrest him. The ex‐Judge, tired of his wrecked 
life, attempted to outdraw Dredd – he was marginally too 
slow and was killed by his former colleague.

## PDF Page 90

ORLOK THE ASsASsIN  
Orlok was born in East‐Meg One and handed over to 
the State Judgment Akademy at the age of four to be 
trained as a Sov Judge. During this training, Orlok’s 
innate physical and mental talents came to the 
attention of his tutors and he was immediately marked 
for special duties by the ruling Diktatoriat of East‐Meg 
One. He would become a vital instrument in their long‐
planned assault on Mega‐City One. In addition to 
receiving training in inﬁltration and assassination, 
enhanced psycho‐indoctrination also resulted in Orlok 
developing an unshakeable loyalty to East‐Meg One.  
When the time was right, Orlok inﬁltrated into Mega‐
City One where he successfully introduced a 
bio‐chemical agent into the city’s water supplies. 
Advanced Sov Judge Training and disciplines made 
Orlok a master of hand‐to‐hand combat, proving to be 
the equal of Judge Dredd in several of their encounters. 
His expertise with ﬁrearms allowed him to outshoot and 
kill many Mega‐City Judges, whilst his superb 
inﬁltration skills enabled him to gain access to 
supposedly secure areas, including Mega‐City One’s 
Weather Control Central. Orlok was perhaps the most 
formidable of Dredd’s purely human opponents and 
was in some respect a warped mirror image of the great 
lawman himself.  
The contaminant Orlok introduced to the City 
stimulated the aggressive, tribal tendencies of humans. 
As the eﬀects spread, the collective psychosis 
christened Block Mania became widespread and the 
city was paralysed as millions of citizens began to ﬁght 
each other. Orlok was ﬁnally overpowered and arrested 
by Judge Dredd, but his mission had been completed 
and a chaotic Mega‐City One was ripe for Sov invasion 
in the ensuing Apocalypse War. Although the 
Apocalypse War eventually culminated with the total 
destruction of East‐Meg One, Orlok survived safe in a 
Justice Department Iso‐Cube from where he swore 
vengeance on his home’s destroyer: Judge Dredd. 
After escaping from the cubes, Orlok continued his 
one‐man war against the Mega‐City and Dredd by 
creating widespread destruction and causing 
thousands of deaths. 
Captured for the ﬁnal time, he was returned to 
Mega‐City One, tried, found guilty, and executed 
via lethal injection, which was watched by a 
record‐breaking Tri‐Vid audience. Some had 
thought that the end of Orlok’s legacy, but 
rumours persist about clones and blood 
relations of the infamous assassin still bent 
on vengeance to the present day.
