## PDF Page 92

FEAR AND LOATHING IN  
MEGA-CITY ONE – CITIZENS  
THE AVERAGE CITIZEN 
The vast majority of Mega‐City One’s teeming 
population live in City Blocks. Thousands of these 
immense 200+ storey habitation buildings dominate 
the city’s busy skyline. A typical three room  
apartment in one of these blocks is expected to 
comfortably house one family of any size in 
reasonable comfort, although some apartments have 
been known to hold up to 20 individuals living in 
oppressively close confinement.  
Most citizens are encouraged to live their entire life 
within the boundaries of their block by its many 
amenities, including shopping malls and communal 
recreation areas. A significant number of citizens 
never even leave their apartments; few have jobs or 
meaningful social ties and the readily apparent 
hazards of life outside their apartment discourage any 
desire to venture far from their homes. Surveys 
conducted by City Hall have determined that 
99.8999% of the Mega‐City’s population would be 
diagnosed as suffering from a profound mental illness 
using early 21st century standards. The results have of 
course been suppressed.  
Isolated behind a usually fortified apartment door 
most citizens live out their life drooling in front of a 
Tri‐D screen, which feeds them a constant diet of 
Justice Department approved reality shows and soap 
operas. Many are hopelessly addicted to low‐level 
psychotropic drugs designed to keep them docile, 
suppressing the stresses of confinement. From the 
Justice Department’s perspective, these chemically 
suppressed citizens are ideal, they are not on the 
streets and they are not committing any crimes. 
Generally, the only time these citizens encounter 
Judges is during a Section 59c routine ‘Crime Blitz’ 
Sweep, when Justice Department personnel enter 
randomly selected apartments to make spot‐checks 
for evidence of wrongdoing.  
90
USING CITIZENS IN SCENARIOS 
Few gunﬁghts and rumbles take place in Mega‐City One without frightened or morbidly curious citizens 
getting in the way. Their sad fate is often to be caught and injured in the mayhem or, more catastrophically, 
becoming ‘meat shields’ getting in the way of heavy gunﬁre. 
If you want to use citizens in your games, then  
the players should agree amongst themselves 
how many citizen models they want to use in 
the scenario.  
Before the ﬁrst turn, players should take it 
in turns to place citizen models on the 
gaming table. To avoid too much 
bunching and avoid nefarious 
players creating ‘human 
shields’ no citizen model 
should start the game 
closer than 2" to any 
other model.


---

## PDF Page 93

AGgRO DOME 
Sudden random acts of aggression appear to be a regular symptom of the crushing drudgery and 
boredom of life in the Big Meg. There comes a point for many citizens where it all becomes too 
much and they embark on an orgy of aggression. This often results in the destruction of property 
and attacks on other citizens or figures of authority. One answer to this was the Aggro Dome. A 
large enclosed area where bored or unstable citizens could take out their anger on specially 
constructed robots and destructible property. No one would get hurt, civic 
property would be safe and the owners would make a fat profit!  
Of course this is Mega-City One and things rarely 
go to plan. Aggro fans soon discovered that 
smashing robots simply was not good 
enough and that the deadly 
weapons they were helpfully 
provided with were just as 
effective against the other 
customers in the dome.  
Before the Judges were able to  
shut it down hundreds of punters 
died in an appalling rampage of 
violence and destruction.

AVERAGE CITIZEN 
SPECIAL RULES 
Apt to Panic 
Citizens don’t always respond well to high‐pressure 
situations. In fact, they can often be relied on to do the 
worst thing possible. At the end of each turn 
roll a Combat dice for each citizen model on 
the table: On a [HIT] they make one normal 
Move action directly away from the closest 
non‐Citizen model. On a [ARMOUR] they take no 
action and remain stationary. On a [SPECIAL], 
they make one Move action towards the 
closest model regardless of type. Citizens will 
move around Blocking terrain, but otherwise 
moves are made without any regard for 
dangers like ledges, gas, ﬁre, rampaging dinosaurs, etc.  
Collateral Damage 
Naturally, citizens are subject to attacks by any Blast or 
other area eﬀects they happen to get caught up in. 
Additionally, any citizen model within 1" of the line of 
ﬁre of a ranged attack is liable to get hit by it. 
Roll a Combat die for each citizen in the line of 
ﬁre: On a [HIT] result resolve the ranged attack 
against the citizen model(s) as well as the 
intended target. Judges are specially trained to 
avoid hitting citizens so only resolve a Judge’s 
ranged attack against a citizen if a [SPECIAL] 
result is rolled. Such an incident will nonetheless 
be thoroughly investigated by the SJS if it does occur, 
of course, so Judges are strongly advised to check their 
ﬁre around citizens.  
Fear the Law 
Any Citizen within 8" of a Judge using the Respect the 
Law skill can be moved by the Justice Department 
player up to one normal Move. The Justice Department 
player also gets to decide whether Citizens that are 
within 8" of a Judge have to roll for Apt to Panic at the 
end of the turn or not. 
Pack 
Citizens do not normally generate Action chips of  
their own. However, if someone else uses an Action 
chip to activate Citizens (representing commanding 
or cajoling them) they follow the pack rules on page 
19; up to six can be activated at once. Unless there are 
mitigating circumstances like Block Mania or ‘angry 
mob syndrome’ in effect, citizens can only select 
Move actions. 
Denizens of mega-city one

## PDF Page 94

THE BLOCK GANGS 
Despite all the comforts and distractions a block 
provides, many young citizens, those generally given 
the tag ‘Juves’, rebel against their conﬁnement. Sheer 
boredom or simple human curiosity pushes them to 
venture outside and travel to other blocks or even 
diﬀerent sectors. Some may look for the escape of 
gainful work, but with almost universal unemployment 
that is generally a fruitless exercise. Some may get 
inducted into the block Citi‐Def unit, especially those 
wanting to live out fantasies based on endlessly 
repeated Tri‐D ‘War Porn’ shows. However, a 
signiﬁcant number are drawn to the criminal life of the 
Block Gang subculture.  
Most gang members get their personal gratiﬁcation 
from simply being part of a larger group, having a sense 
of belonging and joining in with risky criminal activities 
which provide relief from the crushing boredom of 
Mega‐City life.  
Most Juve gang’s criminal activities are relatively petty 
aﬀairs: muggings, apartment break‐ins, and vandalism. 
Many Juves are content to remain operating at this 
level. These crimes are generally beneath the over‐
burdened Justice Department and PSU’s (Public 
Surveillance Unit) notice and a Juve can quickly learn 
that, in many cases, crime does pay. The Juve gangs 
often act as recruitment feeders for the more hardened 
Block Gangs. It is inevitable that the rush of taking 
additional risks, living on the edge and the potential 
lucrative ﬁnancial rewards can pull individuals into 
these organisations. The more ambitious street punks 
may even be encouraged to start their own groups, 
ﬁght turf wars and make a name for themselves in the 
shadowy Mega‐City underworld.  
For most, their careers are short; straying into serious 
crimes such as grand theft and murder quickly earns a 
long stretch in the Iso‐Cubes, or even an Executioner 
round if the ‘Jays’ take notice. Despite this, well‐led and 
organised gangs can survive for a considerable period, 
expanding and growing in inﬂuence until they have 
hundreds of members led by resourceful leaders 
supported by able lieutenants. 
Many gangs identify with, and recruit solely from, a 
single block, whilst others pull in members from all 
across Mega‐City One.  
On occasion, gangs can grow so large that they come to 
the notice of the Council of Five and a full judicial search 
and destroy sweep is ordered, usually resulting in their 
decimation or complete eradication.  
Such an example was the Cosmic Punks, once a fairly 
unremarkable street gang led by the thuggish Gestapo 
Bob Harris. The Punks took advantage of the chaos 
during the reign of Chief Judge Cal, attempting to take 
control of a sizable city sector. Drunk with power, Harris 
foolishly set himself up as the sector’s Judge, where he 
and his lieutenants would act as judge, jury, and 
executioner for anyone else muscling in on their 
territory. After Cal’s fall from grace, Judge Dredd single‐
handedly took down the Cosmic Punks and sentenced 
them to exile in the Cursed Earth for ten years. 
Many gangs will simply protect their own territory 
and try to take control of the local black economy of 
illegal drugs and other banned substances such as 
tobacco, sugar, Umpty Candy and 20th century comic 
books. Other gangs identify with causes or crazes, 
including the many anti‐robot and anti‐mutant outfits 
such as the Neon Knights and Purity First, or anti‐
Judge and pro‐democracy terrorism like the Total War 
group. Others go on to specialise in organised crime 
such as Body‐Sharking, Organ‐Legging, and Alien or 
Perp Running. Organised crime gangs, like Nero 
Narco’s Frendz Mob, can eventually compete with 
92
Blood on the Streets! Judges and Citi-Def 
face an assault by the Cyanidez street gang.


---

## PDF Page 95

93
Denizens of mega-city one
large corporations and their influence can spread off 
world to the interstellar colonies.  
Gangs usually have a quasi‐military organisation with a 
rigid rank structure and advancement based on 
reputation for being a good and loyal money‐earner or 
simply the hardest punk on the block. Larger gangs 
operate to limit the connections between their many 
sub‐sections in order to reduce the chance of a single 
arrest taking down the whole organisation. 
JUVES 
Some Juves enter a life of crime as early as the tender 
age of 6, often acting as pickpockets (‘Dunks’ in Mega‐
City slang) or lookouts for older gang members. They 
tend to be virtual orphans neglected by parents lost in 
the limbo world of Tri‐D addiction. Older Juves become 
their big brothers and sisters, training and beating them 
in equal measures. One of the highpoints of a criminal 
Juve’s life are the ‘Rumbles’, mass semi‐organised 
brawls between rival Juve gangs fought to see who is 
the top dog. 
Although the majority of Juves carry melee weapons, 
older Juves, especially those inducted into a major gang, 
may be given a hand‐me‐down ﬁrearm, which is often 
more dangerous to the shooter than the target. 
PUNKS 
Those Juves who manage to survive past adolescence, 
and have proven themselves of value, can expect to be 
inducted into the ‘dult’ ranks of the gang. Many gangs 
practice convoluted and almost tribal induction rituals 
to secure the candidate’s undying loyalty to the 
organisation. In some gangs, these initiations are so 
extreme that failure usually has lethal consequences. 
These low‐level gang members are generally known as 
Punks and are the foot soldiers of the organisation. 
Usually they are deployed on mugging (‘Tapping’ in 
Mega‐City parlance) or protection racketeering duties. 
However, Punks also act as useful cannon fodder when 
senior gang members require extra muscle for criminal 
jobs they’ve engineered. A favourite tactic is to use 
mass groups of Punks to distract the Justice 
Department whilst more high‐value crimes are being 
carried out elsewhere. Life expectancy among Punks is 
low, but those who survive do so by taking note of their 
betters and honing their criminal skills to the next level. 
HEAVIES  
The Heavies are those gang members who have 
managed to survive in the gang for a number of years 
through exercising their smarts, sheer luck or the 
application of suﬃcient brute force. Heavies are the 
gang’s storm troopers, operating in small groups under 
the control of an able gang lieutenant. When a job needs 
doing, and doing well, the heavies are the gang 
members that the boss relies on to carry it out. Heavies 
receive the best weapons in the gang’s arsenal and the 
pick of any available body armour. Many bosses 
encourage their lieutenants to mould their own retinue 
of heavies as they wish, often with their own 
distinguishing marks worn to complement the parent 
gang’s colours. Some bosses, in the belief it hones their 
ﬁghting ability and toughness, encourage rivalry 
between heavies. Whilst Street Judges can usually 
handle most gang members with little eﬀort, Heavies 
need to be treated with caution as they can pack 
signiﬁcant ﬁrepower with the skills to wield it.  
LIEUTENANTS 
Most gang bosses find it difficult to keep close 
personal control of their gangs once they reach a 
certain size, at which point they often elevate some of 
their followers as lieutenants to whom they delegate 
command of sections of their organisation. Whilst 
some earn their position through merit, being pulled 
from the ranks, others are often the boss’s close 
family members, creating in effect a criminal dynasty. 
Lieutenants coordinate the day‐to‐day activities of the 
gang and are usually permitted to organise heists or 
run their own rackets, as long as they bring in the 
creds and continue to show respect and fealty to the 
boss, of course.  
It’s a well‐accepted fact in the Mega‐City underworld 
that the greatest threat to a boss is not the Justice 
Department, but their own lieutenants. Much of a 
boss’s time is spent closely watching their subordinates 
for any sign of treachery and disloyalty. Countless 
gangs have disintegrated when a power‐grab by a 
lieutenant has broken out into a bloody civil war.

## PDF Page 98

MEAN MACHINE ANGEL 
Mean Machine Angel had the misfortune to be a shy 
and sensitive soul growing up amongst a family of 
violent psychopaths in the badlands of the Cursed 
Earth. The family patriarch, Pa Angel, became so 
appalled by his son’s behaviour that he kidnapped a 
prominent Cyber‐Psycho surgeon from Texas City and 
forced him to perform a radical brain procedure on his 
wayward son. The terriﬁed surgeon burned out those 
parts of the brain governing paciﬁstic tendencies and 
replaced them with implants that would stimulate the 
boy to psychotic violence at the twist of a dial. A metal 
skull plate covers his wounds, featuring a literal dial that 
controls his level of violence. 
In practice, an unfortunate glitch also allows Mean to 
get stuck on a 4 1/2 creating a berserker rage!  
Whilst the surgery also replaced his right arm with a 
Cyber‐Claw, Mean’s signature attack takes the form of a 
charge followed by a devastating head‐butt delivered 
by his metal skull plate.  
Mean Machine’s early criminal career was spent in the 
company of the wider Angel Family gang and he 
perished on the world of Xanadu. However, he was 
brought back to life by Owen Krysler, the Judge Child, 
to become a tool of vengeance against Judge Dredd. 
After being arrested by Dredd he was imprisoned in the 
Justice Department’s high security Iso‐Cube 666. Many 
attempts to cure Mean have failed including the ﬁtting 
of a Warden device punishing aggression with pain. 
Surprisingly, he sometimes leaves the Iso‐Cubes to 
support the Judges in various dangerous missions 
where Mean’s native knowledge of the Cursed Earth is 
vital. Mean is mostly unaware he is helping the hated 
Judges, being duped through drugs or psychic 
manipulation to believe he is still working with his long 
dead family.  
In 2129 Med Judges ﬁnally succeeded in removing both 
his dial and claw, reverting him to his original paciﬁstic, 
self – something even his arch‐nemesis Dredd found 
disturbing. Rumour has it that Mean succumbed to 
violence again under the inﬂuence of the Chaos Bug 
and went back to the Cursed Earth. Only time will tell if 
this crazed individual will turn up again on the streets of 
Mega‐City One.
96
USING MEAN IN SCENARIOS 
Whilst Mean makes an excellent gang faction 
member, during his long periods of 
incarceration in the Iso‐cubes he has been 
borrowed on occasion by the Justice 
Department for particularly dangerous 
missions in the Cursed Earth. However, he has 
usually been hypnotised, drugged or duped to 
see Judges as trusted family members such as 
the deceased Pa Angel. 
For a bit of fun, and assuming both players 
agree, a Justice Department player can take 
Mean Machine as part of their faction. 
However, this can be a dangerous ploy as 
anytime Mean suﬀers a stun or injury result roll 
one Combat die. If the result is [SPECIAL], Mean 
immediately sees through the ruse and swaps 
sides, permanently coming under control of 
the opposing player.

## PDF Page 100

o OLA BLINT – THE ANGEL OF MERCY 
Oola Blint is without doubt the perfect neighbour, a 
caring philanthropist always ready to aid the needy and 
help the wider community. Unfortunately, while you 
may only want to borrow a pack of Munce seasoning, 
Oola has her own agenda on the best way of ‘helping’ 
you. She knows you cannot handle the madness of the 
Mega‐City, she knows you want blessed relief and she is 
more than prepared to be an Angel of Mercy and give 
you that release in the form of involuntary euthanasia!  
Oola is one of the Mega‐City’s most proliﬁc serial killers, 
her many crimes committed in the fervent belief that 
she is doing good. She uses a variety of techniques 
ranging from gas to a meat tenderiser to subdue her 
victims before she ﬁnishes them oﬀ with a lethal dose 
of drugs administered by her Injecto‐Gun. The bodies 
are then left in a state of tranquil repose.  
Oola was able to keep her activities secret for several 
years before ﬁnally being identiﬁed by the Justice 
Department. Fleeing to Brit‐Cit, she opened a legal 
Euthanasiam. However, Oola soon became depressed 
as she came to realise that ending life gave her no 
satisfaction if the victim requested it. After returning to 
the Mega‐City and resuming her old ways, her loving 
husband, and sometimes accomplice, Homer, 
eventually turned her in. They were given life sentences 
in adjoining Iso‐Cubes.

However, Oola’s Injecto‐Gun has been modiﬁed to ﬁre its

HOMER BLINT – BAT GLIDER PEePER 
Whilst Homer Blint may have lived for years in 
ignorance of his beautiful wife’s sinister nocturnal 
serial killer activities, when he finally gained 
enlightenment his devotion, if anything, increased. 
Homer assisted Oola by scouting out likely spots for 
her to ply her deadly trade and then watched her back 
for any inadvertent or unwelcome interventions. As a 
youth he had been involved in the craze of bat gliding, 
revelling in the sensations of silently swooping 
between the gigantic Mega‐City blocks, free as a bird. 
To Homer’s delight, he quickly reasoned that by taking 
to the skies again he could exploit the bird’s eye view 
of the blocks to better watch for unwelcome dangers 
approaching his wife. 
He also found an innovative way of keeping an eye on 
the Justice Department by enrolling as an airborne 
peeper, a ﬂying snitch who would report to the Jays 
any criminal or suspicious activity in the streets below. 
Months in this role enabled him to learn the local 
Judges’ patrol patterns and alert Oola if a crime in the 
vicinity might bring about an encounter. 
So successful were his operations that he became a 
ﬁrm favourite with the local Sector House as a prized 
asset. His skills in bat gliding are second to none and 
his constant tinkering with his gear has pushed its 
performance to the limit. 
 
SPECIAL RULES 
Fly Like an Eagle 
So skilled a ﬂier is Homer that the enhanced attributes 
of his custom Bat Glider are built into his stat line. If this 
model is chosen, Homer is always assumed to be 
airborne and is using the Grav trait during movement. 
Additionally, he is not aﬀected by forcible dismounts. 
Homer’s Eggs  
Homer has developed some custom smoke bombs that 
have a two‐fold purpose. Ostensibly he uses them to 
mark interesting locations when working as a peeper for 
the Judges, but his real incentive for developing them 
was to provide emergency cover for Oola if she needed 
to make a quick escape. 
Homer has the special ability to accurately drop Smoke 
Bombs rather than throw them. At any point during 
Homer’s move, whether a Move or Sprint action, you 
may place a convenient Smoke marker up to 1" away 
from the model. After Homer’s activation has completed, 
models may not trace a line of sight for ranged attacks if 
they pass within 3" of that Smoke marker. 
The eﬀects of the Smoke Bomb remain until the end of 
the game turn it was used in. Remove the Smoke markers 
from the tabletop when you collect up Action chips. 
Homer has enough ‘eggs’ to use on each of his 
activations and requires no Armoury card for their use.

## PDF Page 102

CITI-DEF 
Surprisingly, despite the creation of the Mega‐Cities 
and the implementation of a totalitarian state 
eﬀectively ruled by the Judges, one original 
amendment to the constitution of the old United States 
has been determinedly preserved. The Second 
Amendment states, “A well regulated Militia, being 
necessary to the security of a free State, the right of the 
people to keep and bear Arms, shall not be infringed”. In 
Mega‐City One, this militia is alive and well in the form 
of the City Defence Organisation or ‘Citi‐Def’. Whether 
Citi‐Def is ‘well‐regulated’ is more questionable. This 
organisation, reluctantly viewed by the Justice 
Department as a necessary evil, is very much a two‐
edged sword.  
Each city block in Mega‐City One has the right, if not 
duty, to maintain its own local defence unit.  
Originally, each block Citi‐Def unit was intended to be a 
trained, armed auxiliary composed of well‐balanced 
citizens ready to assist the Justice Department against 
threats both foreign and domestic. Unfortunately, 
whether such a creature as a well‐balanced citizen truly 
exists in Mega‐City One has proven diﬃcult to conﬁrm. 
It has been shown time and again that when average 
citizens with their usual share of grudges, grievances 
and paranoia have access to military grade equipment 
the most mundane law enforcement situations can 
escalate disastrously out of hand. 
A Citi‐Def unit not only has access to personal armour 
and military grade small arms, but each unit will also 
have a nominally secure arsenal of explosives and heavy 
weapons. Some larger blocks even possess last ditch 
strategic defence assets like anti‐missile lasers and 
sonic cannons. Whilst these laser cannons are rather 
commonly turned on rival blocks, the interlocking web 
of multiple Strategic Defence Lasers kept the Mega‐
City largely safe during the Atomic Wars of the  
21st century.  
The average Citi‐Def volunteer is invariably an 
unemployed, disgruntled citizen exposed to blood‐
soaked Tri‐D war favourites like Apocalypse Force and 
the long‐running Bobby Booth’s Boys. Combat‐hungry 
individuals, for whom holo‐computer games just don’t 
scratch the itch, ﬁnd volunteering for Citi‐Def duty 
gives them access to deadly weapons, the training to 
use them, and the chance to satisfy many very 
questionable fantasies.  
This gives the Justice Department two painful headaches. 
Firstly, an ambitious criminal needing access to weapons 
and training need only join the local Citi‐Def. This means 
a substantial part of the Justice Department’s and Public 
Surveillance Unit’s (PSU) activity is focused on tracking 
current or ex‐Citi‐Def volunteers who have the potential 
to go ‘Futsie’ or turn to violent crime. The illegal sale of 
stolen Citi‐Def weaponry can turn a simple turf war or 
criminal escapade into a bloodbath, so all block 
armouries also have to be rigorously monitored and 
frequently audited. 
Secondly, the pressures of Mega‐City life have created 
a new form of mass psychosis: Block Mania.  
Historically, blocks have always been very territorial 
and generally suspicious of their neighbours, choosing 
to believe that the synthi‐grass is always greener in the 
other building. Any problems or hard times that befall a 
block, even if self‐inﬂicted, are usually blamed on the 
interference of other blocks. In most cases, this rivalry 
manifests itself in the form of shouted insults and 
defacement of walls, or through bitterly‐fought inter‐
block contests and sporting events like performance 
eating and pinboing.  
However, tensions can escalate; opposing blockers are 
assaulted and rival block gangs begin turf wars for the 
tweenblock plazas, pipeways and zipstrips that 
100


---

## PDF Page 103

interconnect the blocks. If these incidents are not 
quickly suppressed by the Judges events can rapidly 
spiral out of control with the over‐zealous involvement 
of armed Citi‐Def volunteers itching for action. 
Unauthorised Def Con alerts are declared, blocks 
locked down and Citi‐Def units mobilised; the result is 
a Block War! 
In a Block War a cross between misplaced patriotism, 
war fever, paranoid delusion and mass hysteria takes 
place in the opposing blocks; this is the condition the 
Teks have dubbed Block Mania. While in the grip of 
Block Mania, Citi‐Def squads will battle relentlessly 
against their rivals and anyone that tries to interfere, 
rationalising their foes as traitors, shapeshifting aliens 
or disguised foreign invaders.  
As the combatants are armed with automatic weapons 
and rocket launchers, the whole area can become a 
warzone resulting in hundreds or even thousands of 
deaths. Entire blocks have been brought down by 
determined Citi‐Def demolition teams, each containing 
tens of thousands of warring citizens, which forces the 
Justice Department to resort to extreme measures to 
reassert control. As the Citi‐Def are fond of saying, “if 
you must go out with a bang, make sure that it’s a big 
one. Vape baby, vape!!!!”  
Unfortunately, Block Mania has become recognised  
as a weakness in Mega‐City One that can be exploited 
by its enemies. Both the Apocalypse War and the  
Day of Chaos were preceded by Block Wars provoked 
by East‐Meg agents introducing chemical and 
biological triggers. 
Despite all of the negatives, Citi‐Def squads have 
fought bravely and made the ultimate sacriﬁce 
protecting the Big Meg on countless occasions. During 
the Apocalypse War and Second Robot Wars, the 
Necropolis Crisis, and Judgement Day, Citi‐Def 
performed its intended function by protecting millions 
of citizens when the Justice Department never had 
suﬃcient Judges to do so.  
CITI-DEF LEADERS 
Most Citi‐Def units are commanded by an overall Block 
Captain who, in addition to commanding the unit, also 
has the master key for the block’s heavy weapons and 
the SD laser control room. The unit is generally split into 
squads, each led by a squad leader. It is the quality of 
these squad leaders that usually determine the overall 
eﬀectiveness of a Citi‐Def unit. Whilst some have 
genuine military skills and an aptitude to lead, a 
depressingly large proportion of them just use their 
position as an excuse to strut around and bully other 
citizens for their own gratiﬁcation. Many Citi‐Def Block 
Captains also choose to delegate the access to the 
block armoury to trusted squad leaders. This may allow 
a more rapid distribution of specialist weapons in times 
of crisis, but it can result in heavy weapons being used 
inappropriately or falling into the wrong hands.  
101
Denizens of mega-city one
CITI-DEF TROoPERS 
The rank and ﬁle Citi‐Def trooper is a volunteer drawn 
from the block population who agrees to spend at least 
one day a month attending Citi‐Def training and 
orientation meetings. All are given basic training in 
squad tactics and care and maintenance of personal 
weapons. They are issued with a ballistic shell suit and 
assigned a weapon that must be returned to the 
armoury at the end of any training event. However, 
such is the popularity of the Citi‐Def to certain ‘war‐
crazy’ individuals that Citi‐Def captains can hold 
meetings on a weekly or even daily basis, some even 
allow their enthusiastic followers to take their weapons 
home between sessions – an oﬀence with a mandatory 
sentence of no less than two years per count if the 
Judges ﬁnd out.  
CITI-DEF AUXILIARIES 
Those block citizens not wishing to attend regular 
meetings but still showing an interest in defending the 
block in times of crisis can register as Citi‐Def 
auxiliaries. Whilst usually not issued a formal uniform 
beyond an armband, an auxiliary agrees to attend a 
yearly block Def Con exercise where they receive 
refresher training in weapons use and tactics. In times 
of crisis the auxiliaries can be summoned and issued 
with weapons in support of the regular Citi‐Def.  
CITI-DEF SNIPERS 
The squad leader will usually choose the best 
marksman in his squad to be the designated sniper. 
These men and women tend to be solitary, anti‐social 
types perfectly adjusted to their role. Given a sniper riﬂe 
these individuals will generally ﬁnd a secure hide from 
which to operate. Unfortunately, some take their role 
too seriously and on a number of recorded occasions 
snipers have gone missing for months with only the 
random gunshot deaths of rival block citizens indicating 
their activity. Perhaps the most famous incident was a 
duel between two snipers hailing from Maggie Thatcher 
and Leopoldo Galtieri blocks that lasted over a year with 
nearly 300 civilian casualties. The duel was ﬁnally ended 
when Justice Department Robo‐Hounds were deployed 
to root them out.

BILl BAILEY BLOCK CITI-DEF 
Perhaps one of the most infamous Mega-City One militia units was Bill Bailey block Citi-Def. 
Sealing themselves in a bunker during the initial nuclear exchanges of the Apocalypse War the 
unit believed that the Mega-City had completely fallen to the forces of East-Meg One. The unit 
survived on stockpiled food reserves and prepared obsessively for the time when they would 
return to the surface and bring death to the Sov occupiers, 
never dreaming that their enemies had already been 
destroyed. Years later, they emerged to conduct violent 
guerrilla attacks on the ‘occupying’ garrison of 
Mega-City One Judges and the clearly 
collaborating citizens. The majority of  
Bill Bailey Citi-Def had been driven to the 
edges of sanity by their long isolation. 
Despite attempts to resolve the 
situation peacefully, the troops could 
not be reasoned with and the entire 
Bill Bailey Citi-Def had to be hunted 
down and destroyed. 
CITI-DEF PROFILES 
SPECIAL RULES 
Access to the Armoury 
Larger blocks have a secure armoury in which most of 
the Citi‐Defs weapons are stored. This includes not only 
small arms but also various explosives and an array of 
heavy weapons. Unfortunately, some less than stable 
squad leaders have been known to abuse this authority 
when reacting to a troublesome neighbouring block.  
If a Citi‐Def Squad Leader is the leader of your faction 
when Armoury cards are drawn you draw one extra 
Armoury card and can keep one more card than normal. 
Leading from the Front 
Despite often being as crazy as those they lead, many 
Citi‐Def Squad Leaders possess well‐developed 
leadership skills when coordinating their troops.  
A City‐Def Squad Leader may use a single action to 
return the Action chip of another Citi‐Def model to the 
bag. The target model must be within 8" and line of sight 
of the squad leader. Both actions may be used to return 
the Action chips of two Citi‐Def models to the bag.

with his custom riﬂe have 
honed the Sniper’s 
shooting skills to the 
maximum. Any attempt 
to dodge a hit from the 
Sniper’s ranged attack will be  
at ‐1 Evade.

## PDF Page 106

FATtIES 
Although a relative handful of bored citizens buck the 
trend of sitting in their apartments lost in the grips of 
Tri‐D, joining bat‐gliding clubs, support groups or intra‐
block whist drives (all surprisingly strenuous pastimes), 
the vast majority ﬁnd the mental and physical eﬀort 
required to get oﬀ the couch is just a bit too much.  
Some citizens simply stay put and eat, drifting into 
ultra‐morbid obesity. However, even the most immobile 
still crave a sense of belonging and achievement, even if 
that achievement is being able to eat enough in one 
sitting to keep a Cursed Earth mutie family fed for a 
year. In the 2080s, speed and volume eating, ‘scoﬃng’, 
became a competitive sport and extreme obesity 
became the desired body image. However, for many, 
the goal of becoming a celebrated Fattie was virtually 
impossible due to the post‐Atomic War food restrictions 
or the simple fact that their traitorous metabolisms let 
them down by just keeping them thin!  
This all changed in 2102 when a miracle product of 
Otto Sump’s ugly craze came onto the market. 
Flabbon™ was a food substitute that guaranteed to 
help even the most willowy of frames to gain 300 
pounds in only four weeks! Weights previously 
undreamt of could now be achieved by anyone. 
Popular professional ‘Fatlete’ Tony Tubbs achieved the 
dubious record of being the ﬁrst citizen to break the 
magical two tonne barrier. Since then Fatties 
became an accepted and  
oft‐celebrated facet of Mega‐City society, attracting 
crowds of adoring Fat‐Fans. Unfortunately for the 
corpulent, just as for millions of others, this all changed 
in the aftermath of the Apocalypse War.  
The destruction or irradiation of nearly all stockpiled 
foodstuﬀs and the loss of the majority of the City’s 
nutrient production facilities required the Justice 
Department to impose strict food rationing. This, of 
course, had a catastrophic impact on Fatties who still 
required excessive quantities of chow to maintain their 
lifestyle. Many resorted to begging or queue jumping 
and they pleaded for special treatment. This only 
served to turn large swathes of the population against 
them and a number of Fatties unfortunate enough to 
be caught in the streets were assaulted, killed and, in 
some cases, allegedly eaten by the hungry populace. 
In retaliation, some militant elements of the obese led by 
Chairfatty Dick Porker founded the League of Fatties, who 
believed that they had an inalienable right to food which 
they would take by any means necessary – including 
criminal acts. Whilst an individual Fattie might seem a 
large, ungainly and less than threatening ﬁgure, the ﬁrst 
food raids saw massed ﬂying wedges of obese activists 
trundling forward supported by their BeliWheels®.  
Their layers of ﬂab provide natural armour against 
anything but the most lethal weapons and once 
momentum is gained, little could stop them. Large 
numbers of ‘Skinnys’ and ‘Bones’ were crushed to 
death in the stampedes and kamikaze belly 
ﬂops. However, after the death of several 
Judges trying to protect a food storage 
facility, a crackdown by the Justice 
Department was swift. Most Fatties were 
conﬁned to special Fat Blocks on strict 
diets for the maintenance of public 
order and their own protection until 
their weight fell below the 300‐
kilogram mark.

DICK PORKER 
Dick Porker was the nominal leader of the infamous 
League of Fatties. In the aftermath of the Apocalypse 
War he led the corpulent League in a series of 
coordinated stampede attacks on food distribution

career as a popular guerrilla leader was cut short, as 
was the case with many of his colleagues. The 
importance of making a getaway became secondary to 
the uncontrollable desire to immediately chow down at 
the scene of a crime.  
Dick Porker is currently assisting the Justice 
Department penal division on the design of Fattie‐
speciﬁc Iso‐Cubes and menu content in the hopes of 
earning himself an early release. Fat Chance!

SPECIAL RULES 
Jet Barf 
Many veterans of the Mega‐City One Heavyweight Eating 
Championship are highly skilled in the mysteries of 
Barfology, the science of stomach content management. 
Originally developed as a way of clearing their stomachs 
between ‘Chow Downs’, dedicated contenders have 
perfected projectile vomiting to a ﬁne art.  
Once per game, a Fattie with this rule may perform an 
Aimed Fire double‐action to spray nearby enemies with 
a concentrated stream of corrosive vomit!  
All models caught within the blast will be automatically 
pinned unless they successfully dodge. However, a 
successful jet barf attack will only ever stun. 
Mark the impact point with a convenient marker. For 
the remainder of the game, the area within 2" of the 
impact point is counted as being Diﬃcult terrain. 
Wall of Flub! 
Few sights are more intimidating than a stampeding 
Fattie. Trundling forward, supported by their 
BeliWheel®, a fattie becomes an almost unstoppable 
wall of fat!  When a Fattie makes a Charge move they 
can gain incredible momentum. When determining the 
move distance during a Charge roll 2D6 instead of one, 
but do not add the model’s Move to the result. 
A Fattie must always move the full distance  
calculated above. Mark the end of the Charge with a 
spare dice or counter.  
If during the Charge move a Fattie model moves within 
close combat range (1") of a target it makes a close 
combat attack with a bonus of +2 to its Fight. 
Target models gain +1 Evade to dodge a Wall of Flub! 
attack. (Note that this means that even models with no 
Evade stat get a chance to dodge).  
After the close combat attack is dodged or resolved the 
Fattie will continue to move to the previously marked 
endpoint of their Charge. Whatever the result of the 
close combat, the previous target will be 
displaced 1" out of the path of the Fattie. 
If another model (even a friendly one) is in 
the Fattie’s path when completing the 
Wall of Flub! Charge, another close 
combat attack takes place exactly as 
described above. Multiple models may be 
struck and displaced before the Fattie 
completes their Charge. 
If a Fattie encounters a vehicle or an 
obstacle it cannot move over or ram out 
of the way, a hit is inﬂicted on them using 
the Fattie’s Resist stat as the Power. The 
Fattie’s move is halted, and its Moving 
Fast marker is removed.

## PDF Page 110

FOURTH FACTION AGENTS – SOV GENETIK CONSTRUKTS 
The resurgence of the Soviet Union in the middle years 
of the 21st century, centred on East‐Meg One and Two, 
resurrected old rivalries between the forces of rampant 
consumerism and communism. A second Cold War 
gripped the solar system as both sides gathered huge 
nuclear arsenals dwarﬁng those unleashed in the 
Atomic War. 
The men and women in the New Kremlin however, had 
learned the lessons of history and were convinced that 
only armed struggle and the total subjugation of the 
North American Mega‐Cities would ensure victory. 
Whilst the Apocalypse War very nearly achieved those 
ends, the ﬁnal result was a catastrophic failure and 
countless millions died in the nuclear ﬁreball that 
engulfed East‐Meg One. 
Yet despite this defeat, forces loyal to the Sov dream 
survived. Some were outside the Mega‐City when it was 
destroyed whilst others were deep cover sleeper agents 
living seemingly normal lives in Mega‐City One, hidden 
from the Justice Department and the PSU. All had one 
thing in common – a burning desire to avenge their 
destroyed home. However, these disparate groups 
were initially without leadership, absent after the 
destruction of East‐Meg One.  
At the turn of the 22nd century, East‐Meg One’s ruling 
Dicktatoriat had experimented with sophisticated 
genetic construction techniques to create a race of 
augmented humans called Genetik Construkts. These 
had been intended to replace the low‐quality stock of 
the current proletariat and become the basis of a new 
superior Sov order. In 2104 most of the Genetik 
Construkts, either children or preserved embryos, were 
vaporised in the blast that ended the Apocalypse War. 
However, a small batch survived in a protected bunker 
on the edge of the blast zone and its members were 
raised to maturity by Colonel Yevgeny Borisenko, leader 
of the so‐called ‘Fourth Faction’ of East‐Meg One 
survivors. Amongst his favourites was a young woman 
codenamed Nadia whom he sent to Mega‐City One to 
kidnap the creator of a psychotropic bio‐weapon. 
Borisenko intended to use this against Mega‐City One 
in revenge for the war. 
Upon arriving in the city Nadia made contact with and 
activated a number of sleeper agents to support her 
operations. As with all the Genetik Construkts, Nadia 
had near superhuman abilities, making her 
extraordinarily fast, highly agile and a formidable close 
combat opponent. After completing her mission, she 
remained behind to assassinate Judge Dredd, dying in 
the failed attempt.  
Over the next few months Borisenko activated other 
sleeper constructs, such as comrades Titiana and 
Dmitri, to spread the Chaos Bug and conduct damaging 
hit and run attacks against the Justice Department and 
other Mega‐City institutions. Although it is assumed 
that most of the cells were destroyed during the events 
of Chaos Day, unknown numbers of sleepers are still out 
there on the streets awaiting an activation command. 
Led by a Genetik Construkt, they would pose a 
formidable threat to the depleted Justice Department.  
108

all-girl street Gang at the Dirty Slag Steelworks.

## PDF Page 112

MAN’S BEST FRIEND – THE ROBOTS 
“No more digging, no more drilling, the ﬂeshy ones we’ll 
soon be killing!” 
The main reason for the extremely high rates of 
unemployment in Mega‐City One can be traced to the 
increasing use of robots to perform manually intensive 
tasks during the 21st century. Robots progressed 
rapidly from simple production line units to fully 
ambulatory android models that freely mixed with the 
human population. Despite robots freeing humanity 
from tedious, repetitive, and hazardous activities they 
tend to be regarded with suspicion by a population 
looking for something to blame for their unfulﬁlling 
lives. Of course, if oﬀered a job currently performed by 
a robot many Mega‐City citizens will complain about 
having to do such menial work, generally being too lazy 
to get oﬀ their backsides. 
As robot technologies become increasingly 
sophisticated so too have their Artiﬁcial Intelligence 
routines, advancing in parallel and allowing many to 
achieve either semi or full sentience. This creates both 
philosophical and practical issues; depending on 
personally held views on machine rights, robots can be 
viewed as enslaved sentient beings. 
Most Robots are programmed with ‘AZI‐MOV protocols’ 
that forbid them to harm a human being. Yet, shoddy 
build quality and sometimes‐malicious interference can 
cause a robot to break its programming and run amok. 
From a criminal’s perspective, protocol‐compromised 
robots make ideal accomplices – dedicated, eﬃcient and 
loyal to the last. With suﬃcient planning, assassinations, 
robberies and heists can be undertaken entirely by 
robots that have been carefully purged of any clue to 
implicate their owners. For these reasons, and many 
others, heavy sentences apply to robot tampering and 
manufacturing is strictly monitored. However, a lively 
black market of trade in stolen, illegally imported and 
salvaged robots continues to ﬂourish.  
Despite all precautions, Mega‐City One has been hit 
with two major robot‐related conﬂicts. The ﬁrst Robot 
War of 2099 was initiated by the over‐stimulated 
Artiﬁcial Intelligence routines of the rogue ‘Call‐me‐
Kenneth’ unit. Robots embarked on a costly and bloody 
campaign of extermination against the ‘Fleshy Ones’ 
before Judge Dredd almost singlehandedly ended the 
menace. The second war was precipitated in 2021 by 
the arch‐criminal Nero Narcos, who unleashed hordes 
of Assassinator Droids against the Justice Department 
in his bid to seize control of Mega‐City One. Again, 
casualties were heavy, especially amongst Judges.  
Despite these two major incidents and thousands of 
lesser ones, robots continue to be an inseparable part 
of Mega‐City life. With Mega‐City One’s population 
decimated in the Apocalypse War, Judgement Day and 
the Chaos Bug the presence of robots as a tireless work 
force has become ever more important. 
Unsurprisingly, pro or anti‐robot thinking has created 
great divisions both in the citizenry and in the Justice 
Department. At one extreme, anti‐robot gangs like 
the Neon Knights have carried out attacks against 
robot or pro‐robot spokespersons. At the other end  
of the spectrum, the newly appointed Chief Judge 
Logan showed his pro‐robot sympathies by 
promoting Mechanismo Robot Judge Harvey to a 
position on the Justice Department’s Council of Five. 
This, the adoption of greater numbers of Mechanismo 
robot Judge units and the rise of what agitators 
derisively call ‘Machine‐Law’ have caused a worrying 
increase in protests and disorder despite the 
impressive statistics of robot Judges when it comes to 
quelling lawlessness. 
110
USING ROBOTS IN GAMES 
It’s not uncommon to see MediBots and UtilBots 
backing up Justice Department operations, and for 
certain tasks like bomb disposal they are 
indispensable. Criminal organisations, Citi‐Def 
units and other groups will generally use whatever 
robots they can co‐opt into their service unless 
they hold strong anti‐robot sentiments. Robots can 
be added into any faction as helpers, auxiliaries 
and specialists for the requisite Notoriety cost.  


---

## PDF Page 113

There are probably millions of UtilBots of diﬀerent 
shapes and sizes operating in Mega‐City One. These 
relatively unsophisticated machines are commonly 
used as domestic servants for cleaning, cooking, and 
other duties deemed too taxing for the average citizen. 
The majority are City Hall owned ‘bots that patrol the 
streets, blocks, and communal spaces to clear them 
of the daily build‐up of refuse and perform general 
maintenance tasks. Occasionally a UtilBot will break its 
programming and go rogue. Whilst most incidents can 
be quickly dealt with, a crazed domestic robot 
brandishing supposedly harmless household cleaning or 
food preparation implements can be almost as 
dangerous as a fully tooled up security‐bot.

without insurance and then demand payment for

Whilst the Justice Department is supposed to 
maintain law and order in the Mega‐City their 
resources cannot cover general security and 
protection of property. It is possible to simply hire 
human security personnel for those tasks but they 
usually prove all too fallible and prohibitively 
expensive, especially if 24‐hour coverage is required. 
SecBots are the economical answer. These machines 
are generally uprated UtilBots armed with non‐lethal 
close and ranged weaponry. The Justice Department 
provides licencing for these units but woe‐betide the 
owner of a SecBot that seriously injures or kills a 
criminal in its duties; a long stretch in an Iso‐Cube is 
the most likely result.

targets when using Stun weapons.

Despite the many warnings of intellectuals about the 
dangers of robot ﬁghting machines Robert L. Booth, 
the last president of the United States, let the genie out 
of the bottle in the late 2060s. Cornered, and unwilling 
to step down from oﬃce after triggering the 
devastating Atomic Wars, Booth unleashed his robot 
army, known as the Legion of the Damned, against his 
enemies in the Justice Department. A bitter civil war 
was fought culminating in his defeat at the Battle of 
Armageddon in 2071. 
WarBots have been banned in North America since 
Booth’s time but they have never been fully eradicated. 
Some have emerged as leftover relics from the Civil War 
while others have been built covertly by government 
agencies, private individuals or other organisations. 
Several of the other Mega‐Cities are less wary about 
WarBots; the Sov‐block continued to use dedicated 
combat robots right through the Apocalypse War. 
Many point to the Justice Department’s Mechanismo 
program as being a continuance of the WarBot line, and 
Nero Narcos’ Assassinator Droids certainly ﬁtted the 
description. A WarBot is frighteningly self‐aware, 
heavily armoured and usually armed to the teeth. With 
an arsenal of deadly weaponry, its entire purpose is to 
ﬁght and it seems to derive an analogue of satisfaction 
from doing so.

Whether from the eﬀects of Sov nukes, block wars, or 
simple shoddy building standards, the urban landscape 
of Mega‐City One is in constant ﬂux. At any time, night 
or day, hundreds of derelict or damaged buildings are 
being torn down to make room for new habs to house 
the city’s teeming population. At the heart of these 
operations are teams of large demolition robots 
capable of taking down a 200‐storey block in the space 
of a single day.  
Such is the spectacle of these operations that large 
building demolition has become something of a main 
event for the city’s bored civilians. Hundreds or even 
thousands have been known to gather to see the ‘bots 
at work. No one quite knows when the bots became 
widely known as the Heavy Metal Kids but the name 
has stuck. Some individual bots have become so 
popular they have their own fan clubs and avid 
followers. Rumours abound of unregistered DemoBot 
ﬁghts in the bowels of the under‐city but even the PSU 
has failed to ﬁnd any evidence. 
During the 2099 Robot War, the name Heavy Metal 
Kid became synonymous with terror when many of 
these machines became the bodyguard of the rogue 
robot terrorist Call‐Me‐Kenneth. Even the strongest 
defensive barricades proved futile when the Kids were 
let loose and many citizens and Judges died as a result. 
Too valuable to ban, the DemoBots are now required by 
law to be controlled with triple lock inhibitors but even 
with these precautions ‘malfunctions’ still happen often, 
with predictably messy consequences. Although 
DemoBots make ponderous combatants, criminal 
organisations and rogue Citi‐Def squads can ﬁnd plenty 
of uses for a virtually invulnerable walking wrecking ball.

## PDF Page 118

NERO NARCOS – ROBOT OVERLORD 
Nero Narcos was the boss of the criminal syndicate 
known as the Frendz Mob, possibly the most powerful 
criminal organisation in Mega‐City One. Nero became a 
cyborg after barely surviving an assassination attempt. 
His undamaged brain was transplanted into a heavily 
armoured robotic body, after which he became obsessed 
with taking total control of Mega‐City One and the 
complete destruction of the Justice Department. Frendz 
controlled several high‐tech industrial plants and 
laboratories across the planet, which allowed Narcos to 
develop tools to assist him in his goal. 
The main instruments of Narcos’ power were thousands 
of heavily armed Assassinator Droids, which he kept in 
numerous hidden caches across the city. Narcos was also 
smart enough to know that, despite being outnumbered, 
the Judges would be his greatest threat, so he hatched an 
insidious plot to neutralise them. One of the Frendz many 
Trojan companies had been given the contract to supply 
certain key components for the Justice Department’s new 
Lawgiver Mk2 pistols. These parts were fundamental in 
controlling the Lawgivers’ self‐destruct protocols. When 
Operation Doomsday opened with a mass attack by 
Assassinator Droids on the forces of the Justice 
Department, hundreds of Lawgivers exploded in the 
hands of their owners crippling all ability to ﬁght back.  
The Judges were driven into hiding and Narcos gained 
control of the Mega‐City’s nuclear arsenal, becoming the 
city’s de‐facto ruler. The intervention of Judge Dredd 
eventually caused Narcos’ downfall. He succeeded in 
locating the Frendz reserve of Assassinator Droids in the 
Cursed Earth, and had the Droids reprogrammed to serve 
the remaining Judges as auxiliaries. Assisted by a group of 
Brit‐citizens, Judge Dredd’s robot army destroyed Narcos’ 
forces and cornered the evil mastermind. Narcos 
remained deﬁant to the end and was executed by the 
mass ﬁre of a dozen vengeful Judges.

SPECIAL RULES 
Robot Control  
Narcos had the technical means to take control of 
Robots and direct them to do his bidding. When Narcos 
is activated he can use one or both of his actions to 
allow any friendly robot that has already activated to 
perform a bonus single action. If he uses both actions 
for this purpose, he may give bonus single actions to 
two diﬀerent robots. However, no single robot can be 
given two actions. 
Self-Repair Sub-Routines 
Nero’s body is capable of rapid and highly eﬀective  
self‐repairs. If Narcos is Injured and uses the Hunker 
Down double‐action, it can potentially remove all Injury 
markers if enough successes are rolled. 
Who Needs Frendz 
Any force led by Nero Narcos has the resources, power 
and inﬂuence of the Frendz mob behind it. The 
weapons and technology of their opponents may 
simply stop working due to Trojan glitches or malicious 
hacking. If Nero is the leader of your faction then after 
both factions have drawn their Armoury cards, you may 
randomly pick and discard one of your opponent’s 
Armoury cards.  
WEAPONS 
The FI‐27 is an ultra‐compact grenade launcher is a 
product of one of the Frendz mob’s industrial assets. It 
utilises designs and components stolen from the Judges’ 
Lawgiver technical blueprints, creating a fearsome and 
versatile weapon. 
Special Rounds 
The use of special rounds requires the expenditure of 
speciﬁc Armoury cards: 
In all cases, a Special Round is ﬁred using the short and 
long range Shoot modiﬁers of the FI‐27.  
Special Rounds always require an Aimed Fire double‐
action and do not get additional Aimed Fire beneﬁts. 
Normally when an Armoury card is expended it is 
discarded. However, if the card has been used  
for an FI‐27 shot there is a chance that the card is not 
immediately discarded. After each special 
round shot, roll a Cool test for the ﬁring model. 
If any result is a [SPECIAL] then the card is 
retained and may be used again. The card can 
potentially be used multiple times in a game 
turn as long as it keeps getting retained.

## PDF Page 120

118
GREeTINGS FROM THE  
CURSED EARTH – MUTIE SKY RAIDERS  
The Atomic Wars of the 21st century devastated large 
areas of North America. Almost all of the cities and 
entire swathes of the countryside were blasted into 
inhospitable and highly‐radioactive wasteland by 
showers of nuclear missiles. Only the three Mega Cities 
(Mega‐City One and Two, plus Texas City) survived this 
fate thanks to their foresight of installing Strategic 
Defence Laser networks to protect themselves from 
incoming missiles. As the war sputtered out ,the Mega‐
Cities became the only refuge for desperate survivors 
ﬂeeing from the nuclear hell of what quickly became 
known as the Cursed Earth. Initially, many were given 
shelter before a growing fear of these irradiated 
individuals produced a deadly backlash. 
Whilst the most seriously irradiated quickly succumbed 
to the eﬀects of the bombing those that survived were 
viewed with suspicion and horror. Public opinion was 
inﬂamed by rumours that somehow these survivors 
could pass on the eﬀects of radiation poisoning as if it 
were an infectious disease. Persecution became all the 
more hysterical when the ﬁrst children of pregnant 
survivors were born manifesting visible mutations. In 
most cases the authorities turned a blind eye to the 
persecution of ‘Muties’ as they already had their hands 
full keeping the Mega‐City infrastructure functional and 
controlling civil disturbances over food, water and living 
space. Eventually each Mega‐City enacted draconian 
measures forcing their Mutie populations out of the city 
and back into the countryside. 
The majority of these unfortunates soon died 
out, but some were able to set up basic 
communities and adapt to the horriﬁc 
conditions in the Cursed Earth. These ﬂedgling groups 
often kept themselves isolated, shunning any contact 
with the old world. Many have maintained an undying 
grudge against their persecutors and take every 
opportunity to strike at the hated Mega‐Cities. Most are 
content to opportunistically ambush any Mega‐City 
assets that venture outside the city walls. However, 
more radical Mutie factions dream of taking the Mega‐
Cities away from the ‘norms’ and take every opportunity 
to raid the cities themselves, causing destruction and 
sowing terror. 
Due to the massive wall defences of the Mega‐Cities an 
overland attack is nigh suicidal. Raiders who master the 
air have had the most success, suddenly swooping over 
the wall defences on a bewildering array of jet‐packs, 
skyboards and anti‐grav transports. Despite the 
widespread opinion of Muties as stupid and animal‐like, 
a surprising number are tech‐savvy and scavenge 
extensively among the ruins of the Cursed Earth. There 
are dozens of small bands with the skill to convert run‐
of‐the‐mill mounts, such as Power Boards and Sky 
Bikes, into deadly machines of war.  
Attacks by these so‐called Mutant Sky Raiders have 
been on the increase and continue to be a big  
problem for the Justice Department. Their hit and run 
raids can strike anywhere along the thousands of miles 
of city walls, making it diﬃcult for the Judges to 
concentrate their forces quickly enough to mount an 
eﬀective response. Punitive expeditions mounted into 
the Cursed Earth are risky and usually fruitless as they 
fail to ﬁnd the elusive raiders. 
An additional headache for the Justice 
Department is that certain Mega‐City gangs, 
some of whom already have sympathies 
with the mutant cause, are often willing 
to assist these deadly raids with intel, 
weapons and even reinforcements. 
Whether they are seen as 
renegade mutant horrors, 
terrorising the innocent, or 
downtrodden exiles ﬁghting for 
their existence, a Mutie raid 
makes great cover for all sorts of 
nefarious schemes to occur while 
the Judges’ backs are turned.

SPECIAL RULES 
Mutie Insight 
It might be a psychic seer, raw instinct or help from 
inside the city, but Mutie raids often coincide with 
chaos, disorder and other seemingly ‘random’ events. 
If a Sky Raider Pack Leader is the leader of your faction 
when Big Meg cards are drawn, you draw one extra Big 
Meg card and can keep one more card than normal. 
Scary Mutant 
Most Cursed Earth mutants gain no direct beneﬁts from 
their mutations, but they are nevertheless fearsome, 
weird or just plain freaky to look at. 
Models other than Judges suﬀer ‐1 on any tests made 
against their Cool stat when they are within 4" of a Mutie.  
Note: if you are looking to do more with mutations, 
guidelines and stats can be found in the Strontium  
Dog rulebook. 
Take That, Norms! 
A Sky Raider pack is a tightly bound group of ﬁghters 
used to ﬁghting alongside each other. The lynchpin is 
the Pack Leader. 
When the Sky Raider Pack Leader activates, they can 
use a single action to remove D3 Action chips from any 
Sky Raiders within 6" and return those chips to the bag.

## PDF Page 124

CHOoSING A SCENARIO 
A number of scenarios are presented here. However, if 
you’re playing the game for the ﬁrst time it can be best 
to start with the earlier ones as they are simpler. 
Choose a scenario either by agreement with your 
opponent, rolling a dice or simply picking one that 
sounds fun. If you want a fairly straight forward ﬁght 
then pick Scenario 3: Rumble. 
A scenario comprises three elements: the Set Up, the 
Encounter, and the Sentencing.  
122
OVERVIEW 
Encounters between Judges and criminals occur every 
second of every day in Mega‐City One. Not all of these 
encounters result in violence, but many do and (generally) 
the more serious the crime being perpetrated, the more 
likely the perpetrators are to try and evade justice. Even 
aside from day‐to‐day judicial encounters, all manner of 
criminals and crazies target one another in street ﬁghts, 
assassination attempts, thefts and property destruction – 
usually provoking a Justice Department response that is 
both swift and merciless. 
The following scenarios reﬂect diﬀerent ways two factions 
can end up ﬁghting each other. You might be playing a 
straight‐up ﬁght between two crazy Citi‐Def units, a Judge 
tasked with taking out a dangerous perp, or 
two rival gangs trying to corner the fake 
Umpty Candy market. To cover such a 
wide variety of situations we use a few 
basic scenarios to set up diﬀerent 
kinds of encounters and let the 
player’s imagination (and model 
collection) do the rest.

## PDF Page 125

THE SET UP  
The Set Up indicates the suggested size of forces to be 
used in the scenario, expressed as a number of Notoriety 
points to spend on models. Most players prefer to select 
their forces ahead of choosing the scenario.  
Set-up sequence 
1. Choose your Faction 
2. Draw Armoury cards 
3. Draw Big Meg cards  
4. Deploy models 
5. Start Playing 
1. CHOoSE YOUR FACTION 
The scenario will oﬀer some guidelines whether it is 
suitable for small, medium or large games expressed as 
a number of Notoriety Points for the opposing factions. 
Precisely what forces you decide to use is between you, 
your opponent, your model collection and your 
imagination; gang versus gang, block versus block, 
Judge versus Judge? You can even use aliens, robots, 
mutants, undead, werewolves (yes really) – Mega‐City 
One has seen it all. The scenarios that follow are written 
with the default concept of being suitable for Judges 
versus criminals, but they are written with an 
eye to ﬂexibility of opposing forces. 
2. DRAW ARMOURY CARDS 
Each player is dealt six Armoury cards at random from 
the Armoury card deck and chooses three to keep for 
use in the coming ﬁght. These cards are kept secret, 
and the others are discarded without being revealed. 
Example: Karl is dealt the Hi‐Ex, Stumm Gas, 
Respirator, Incendiary, Cuﬀs and Med‐Pack 
Armoury cards. Karl discards the Peepers, Hi‐Ex, 
and Respirator, keeping the other three.  
3. DRAW BIG MEG CARDS 
Players are each dealt three Big Meg cards from the Big 
Meg deck. Certain skills or encounter events may give a 
player additional Big Meg cards. These cards can be used 
as explained in their individual rules for certain beneﬁts.  
Example: Mike deals himself three Big Meg cards 
and gets Sixth Sense, Flanking Move and Crazie! 
4. DEPLOY MODELS 
Place the scenery according to the entry for the 
Encounter and the advice in the Scenery section of this 
book. The players deploy their models on the tabletop 
as described in the Encounter they are playing.  
5. START PLAYING 
Count up the number of Action chips 
for each side, put them in the bag, 
and start the ﬁght!

## PDF Page 126

THE ENCOUNTER 
The Encounter tells you where the opposing sides meet 
each other and what their scenario objectives are. It also 
includes the placement of terrain and any other special 
rules or restrictions for the models involved.  
The Encounter includes the turn‐by‐turn game itself, 
resolved as per the Core Rules section. At the end of 
each turn the players check to see if one or both sides 
have achieved their objective.  
SENTENCING 
Sentencing occurs when the game is complete as a 
kind of epilogue. Any perpetrators caught by the 
Judges will be sent to the Iso‐Cubes (prison) for the 
appropriate sentences for their crimes. An accounting 
is also done of how many injured there are to call for 
the med‐wagons and the number of deceased 
heading to Resyk. 
At the end of the game roll a Resist test for 
each incapacitated model, if they fail to roll a 
[ARMOUR] result they get a ride on the meat 
wagons to Resyk, to be rendered into a variety 
of useful organic compounds for the city. 
Judges count both [SPECIAL] and [ARMOUR] as a 
success when they make this roll. Any perps 
that survived being incapacitated, and any 
subdued models, get med‐wagons and 
sentencing. Surviving Judges will be back on 
the streets within 24 hours.
Outlines for crimes and sentencing are listed on pages 
126‐127. Sentences might be ﬁxed (e.g. 20 years) 
randomised (e.g. D6+2 years) or variable (e.g. 1 month 
to two years or 20 years to Life). Where variable 
sentencing applies it is at the discretion of the arresting 
Judge. In some cases the scenario will oﬀer additional 
randomised sentences for certain crimes.  
Keep in mind that multiple crimes can and will stack up 
as multiple counts for sentencing (e.g. get caught with 
six wallets that aren’t yours; that’s six counts of dunking 
or receiving stolen goods, possibly both) so perps can 
ﬁnd themselves sent to the cubes for a long, long time. 
Sentencing in one‐oﬀ games is just fun to know. However, 
in the context of a campaign it is useful to understand 
when a criminal might be released, or more likely how 
securely they are going to be held when their compatriots 
decide to try and bust them out. A high security Iso‐Cube 
facility in the city that’s designed to withstand nukes is a 
very diﬀerent prospect to a Cursed Earth penal farming 
colony under intermittent mutie raider attack. 
It’s usually more practical to keep track of sentencing, 
or at least the crimes, as they occur instead of trying to 
recall everything at the end of a game. Players of 
Judges in particular are encouraged to declare 
sentences on crimes as they are committed and keep 
notes on the perpetrators. 
E.g. Karl: ‘Ok I take a Snapshot action at your Judge 
with my Juve over here.’  
Jane: ‘THAT’S THREE TO EIGHT YEARS FOR 
RESISTING ARREST, CREEP. BETTER HOPE YOU 
MISS THAT SHOT, PUNK, OR YOU’RE LOOKING AT 
ANOTHER TEN FOR ASSAULTING A JUDGE.’ 
124

## PDF Page 127

CHUMP DUMPING 
Perp-runners offer their services to smuggle wanted perps out of Mega-City One, usually to one  
of the off-world colonies or other alien planets. While these outward journeys are very 
profitable with all costs being covered by the running perp’s 
fare, the return trips also need to 
be financed. Aware of the millions 
of aliens who want to emigrate 
to Earth, many unscrupulous 
perp-runners offer to smuggle 
aliens into Mega-City One which 
they exaggerate to be an alien 
paradise. Of course the reality is 
that getting past immigration 
controls is well-nigh impossible. 
The perp-runners will therefore 
take exorbitant payment for the 
trip to Earth before dumping the 
hapless aliens into the vacuum 
between worlds.

## PDF Page 128

126
CRIMES AND SENTENCING 
Section 1: Homicide 
• Premeditated* murder of a Judge during the 
execution of their duty: Life 
• Premeditated* mass murder (spree or serial 
killing): 30 years to Life 
• Premeditated* murder: 15 years to Life 
*Judicial considerations of premeditation are 
liable to be extremely broad and will include just 
having the means, motive and/or opportunity. 
In game terms any time a model is incapacitated there’s 
a chance it will be murder. See scenario sentencing for 
survival rolls. 
Section 2: Assault 
• Assault on a Judge resulting in actual or grievous 
bodily harm: 10 years 
• Assault on a citizen resulting in grievous bodily 
harm: D6+4 years 
• Assault on a citizen resulting in actual bodily 
harm: D6 years 
• Torture: 2D6+3 years 
In game terms, inﬂicting an Injury marker is grievous 
bodily harm and stuns are actual bodily harm.  
Section 3: Robbery 
• Armed robbery with violence: 5D6 years 
• Armed robbery: 4D6 years 
• Hijacking: 3D6+2 years 
• Tapping (mugging): 5D6 years 
• Wrecking: 4D6 years
Section 4: Burglary 
• Burglary: 3D6+2 years 
Section 5: Theft &  
Theft-Related Oﬀences 
• Shoplifting: D6‐1 years (minimum 1 year) 
• Dunking (pick‐pocketing): D3+1 years 
• Theft: 2D6 years 
• Dealing in stolen goods: D6‐1 years (minimum  
1 year) 
• Possession of stolen property: D6‐1 years 
(minimum 1 year) 
In game terms robbery, burglary and theft charges 
are liable to come from the reasoning for the scenario 
rather than actions during the game itself, although 
hijacking might come into play. 
Section 6: Arson 
• Setting ﬁres with intent to cause death: Life 
In a game using incendiary weapons will be suﬃcient 
to earn this sentence. 
Section 7: Medical & Scientiﬁc 
• Unauthorised possession of controlled biological 
or chemical substance: 15 years to Life 
• Body Sharking: 15 years to Life 
• Organ Legging: 15 years to Life 
• Body Snatching: Life 
These charges are liable to arise in games involving 
cache markers, see the Heist and the Raid scenarios 
for details. 
Section 8: Criminal Damage 
• Robot Smashing: 3D6 years 
• Criminal Damage: 2D6‐1 years 
• Vandalism: D6 years 
• Scrawling (graﬃti) : 1 month to 1 year 
In a game, criminal damage charges are possible every 
time a perp discharges a ﬁrearm, and mandatory if 
they use an explosive device or heavy weapon.


---

## PDF Page 129

127
Scenarios
Section 9: Illegal  
Imprisonment & Abduction 
• Abduction: 15 years to Life 
• False imprisonment: D3x10 years 
• Kidnap (abduction with ransom demand):  
20 years to Life 
• Chump Dumping (robbing & murdering 
passengers): Life 
• Hostage taking: 3D6+2 years 
These charges are liable to arise in games involving 
cache markers, see the Heist and the Raid scenarios 
for details. 
Section 10: Technology 
• Implanting aggression chips into robots: 5D6 years 
• Illegal possession or trading of WarBots or 
Assassinator Droids: 3D6 years 
• Unauthorised construction of weapons of mass 
destruction: 10 years to Life 
These charges are most liable to arise in games 
involving cache markers, see the Heist and the Raid 
scenarios for details. Players with a fondness for 
including WarBots or Assassinator Droids in their 
force may run afoul of these laws too. 
Section 11 Public Order 
• Block War participation: 15 years to Life 
• Rioting: 3D6 years 
• Unlawful assembly: 3 months to 1 year 
• Drunk and disorderly: Compulsory rehab +2 years 
Judges will be able to level one of these charges 
against perps in most games with little diﬃculty. 
Section 12: Public Nuisance 
• Boinging® in a public place: 20 years ﬁxed 
• Low level or dangerous sky surﬁng/bat gliding: 
5D6 years 
• Littering: Cr. 100 ﬁne or up to 1 year; D6 years 
for a repeat oﬀence 
• Peeping: 4D6 months 
• Loitering: 2D6 years 
• Jaywalking: 4D6 months 
• Begging: 2D6 months 
• Intentional wasting of Justice Department time: 
2D6 months 
• Smoking in public: D3 years + compulsory detox 
Most of these charges are minor but oﬀer plenty of 
scope for a Judge looking for extra charges to apply. 
Boinging® and dangerous ﬂying in public are 
particularly heinous crimes however, and perps 
should be prosecuted fully on those counts. 
Section 13:  
Impersonation & Deception 
• Jimping (impersonation of a Judge) with intent 
to commit crime: 3D6+2 years 
• Unauthorised impersonation of a celebrity or 
public ﬁgure: 2D6 months 
• Impersonation of a known criminal: 4D6 months 
These charges may come into play from Big Meg 
cards and scenario narratives. 
Section 14: Contraband 
• Possession or manufacture of illegal substance 
with intent to trade: 5D6 years 
• Possession of illegal substance with intent to use: 
2D6‐2 years (minimum 1 year) + compulsory detox 
• Illegal or unlicensed ﬁrearm: 5D6 months. 
• Umpty bagging (addictive candy distribution): 
5D6 years 
• Possession or trade of restricted publications: 
D6 years 
Most of these charges are liable to arise in games 
involving cache markers, see the Heist and the Raid 
scenarios for details. However, possession of illegal 
or unlicensed ﬁrearms is a charge that can be levelled 
at just about any armed model that isn’t a Judge. 
Section 15: Pollution 
• Creating atmospheric pollution: Fine or up to  
2 years 
• Noise pollution: Fine or up to 2 years 
Using gas, smoke and incendiaries can net a  
perp these charges. Gunﬁre can be interpreted as 
noise pollution. 
Section 16:  
Perverting the Course of Justice 
• Resisting Arrest: D6+2 years 
• Obstructing a Judge in their duty: 3D6 years 
• Escaping from Justice Department custody or 
imprisonment: 4D6 years 
• Perp running (aiding an escape out of Mega‐City 
One): 5D6 years 
Refusing to surrender when challenged is resisting 
arrest, ﬁghting back is deﬁnitely obstructing a Judge 
in their duty.

## PDF Page 130

THE SET UP 
Foot Chase is best suited to small and medium sized 
games. Vehicles are not permitted except by prior 
agreement between the players. It is not essential that 
Judges be involved in this scenario – one gang may 
simply have caught sight of the other across a crowded 
ped‐way and started chasing them. If Judges are one of 
the opposing factions they will always be the pursuers. 
If no Judges are involved (or Judges on both sides) 
players roll a dice each and the high roller is the pursuer. 
128
FOoT CHASE
SCENARIO 1
FOOT CHASE SCENARIO MAP
CENTRAL  
STREET
SIDE STREETS
SIDE STREETS
CHASED  
MODELS
THE ENCOUNTER 
Terrain Placement 
The table must include a central street 12"‐18" wide  
and running across the table from one side to the other. 
The street should be open and clear of any buildings but 
could still include barriers, fast‐food stands, benches (or 
other seating areas), trash compactors, parked vehicles, 
etc. The areas to the sides of the main street should have 
buildings with plenty of interconnecting cross streets 
and alleyways. 
Deploy Models 
The player being chased deploys all their models within 
6" of the centre of the table. The chasing player then 
chooses one end of the central street and deploys their 
models within 1" of the table edge.  


---

## PDF Page 131

Big Meg Cards 
As this chase takes place in a thoroughfare, the full 
craziness of the Mega‐City is in eﬀect. Both players 
draw one additional Big Meg card after all models have 
been deployed. 
Special Rules 
Surprise! 
The side that is being chased has been caught off‐
guard and may momentarily freeze up or simply not 
notice the approaching peril. Before the game starts 
the chasing player nominates D6 opposing models to 
take Cool tests. Any that fail the test begin the 
scenario Pinned. 
Back-Up 
If any models of the chasing force are incapacitated or 
subdued do not remove their Action chip from the bag 
at the end of the turn. A replacement model of equal or 
lesser Notoriety will be deployed at the beginning of 
the next game turn. The model appears at the same end 
of the street where the chasers appeared at the 
beginning of the game. 
Trip hazard 
The clutter of Mega‐City One often gets underfoot. If 
any models in the force being chased attempts to Sprint 
roll two D6 instead of one and take the lowest result. If 
both dice rolled are the same result the model stumbles 
and only gets a basic Move instead of the normal 2x 
Move + D6". 
Leaving the Table 
Models being chased can leave the table if they  
move across a table edge by any means. However, 
models attempting to leave the table from anywhere 
except the central street may blunder into a dead end 
or fence just as they think they’re safe; roll a 
Combat die for them and on result of [HIT] they 
move to the edge of the table  
and no further this activation.  
Game Length 
The game continues until all models being chased have 
left the tabletop, been incapacitated or subdued. 
Objectives 
The side being chased needs to get away as they are 
ﬁghting at a severe disadvantage. If a number of 
models equal to half or more of the original force 
(rounding up) escapes they can claim victory. Models 
can escape by moving oﬀ any table edge. If the chasing 
force manages to incapacitate or subdue more than half 
of the models being chased they can claim victory. 
SENTENCING 
Any model being sentenced will automatically receive a 
minimum of D6 years for possession of contraband, 
unlicensed weaponry, overdue vid‐slugs, running in a 
no‐running zone, and the like, in addition to the listed 
sentences. The following sections of the Book of Law 
will likely apply in a foot chase: 
• Section 1: Homicide; Section 2: Assault; Section 8: 
Criminal Damage; Section 11 Public Order; Section 12: 
Public Nuisance; Section 16: Perverting the Course  
of Justice.

AMBUSH
SCENARIO 2

## PDF Page 133

131
THE SET UP 
Ambush is best suited to small and medium sized 
games. Vehicles are permitted in an ambush scenario.  
Sometimes what starts oﬀ as a chase will actually lead 
into an ambush, as the over‐eager pursuers are drawn 
into hostile territory. Other times an ambush is a more 
clinical, planned aﬀair designed to trap and take down 
the opposition with as little risk as possible for the 
attackers. Judges in particular are fond of springing 
ambushes on perps to catch them red‐handed in 
criminal acts. Conversely, the more bloodthirsty and 
violent gangs will ambush Judges to prove their native 
ferocity and lack of long‐term aspirations about living. 
If one of the factions has more models than the others 
they will be the ones getting ambushed. If both players 
have an equal number of models roll oﬀ and the low 
roller gets ambushed. 
THE ENCOUNTER 
Terrain Placement  
The table must include a cross roads with streets 12"‐
18" wide running across the table from each side to the 
other. The street should be open and clear of any 
buildings but could still include barriers, fast‐food 
stands, benches and other seating areas, trash 
compactors, parked vehicles, etc. The areas in the four 
corner sections should have buildings with vantage 
points overlooking the crossroads. 
Deploy Models 
The player being ambushed deploys all their models 
within 6" of the centre of the table. The ambushing 
player deploys half (rounding up) of their models so 
they are further than 9" from any enemy models.  
Special Rules 
Ambush! 
Before the game starts the ambushing player takes 
Cool tests for all of their deployed models. Any that 
pass the test begin the scenario in Overwatch. 
Back-Up 
The ambushing player’s remaining models are nearby 
and ready to strike after the ambush is sprung. Include 
the Action chips for all of the ambushing models in the 
bag. When a model that isn’t on the table is activated it 
must take a Move action to move on from any table 
edge as its ﬁrst action before choosing its second 
action. A double‐action may not be chosen when 
entering the table.
Leaving the Table 
Models being ambushed can leave the table if they 
move across a table edge by any means. Ambushing 
models that leave the table for any reason can return 
using the Back‐Up rules.  
Game Length 
The game continues until all models being  
ambushed have left the tabletop, or have been 
incapacitated or subdued. 
Objectives 
The side being ambushed needs to survive. If a number 
of models equal to half or more of the original force 
(rounding up) survives they can claim victory.  
The ambushing force needs to take down their targets. 
If the ambushing force manages to incapacitate or 
subdue more than half (rounding up) of the models 
being ambushed they can claim victory. 
SENTENCING 
The following sections of the Book of Law may apply in 
an ambush.  
• Section 1: Homicide; Section 2: Assault; Section 6: 
Arson; Section 8: Criminal Damage; Section 11:  
Public Order; Section 13: Impersonation & Deception; 
Section 16: Perverting the Course of Justice.
